Bank counts include the 30 constructed questions. "Bank only" means the type exists in the third-party banks; "Unverified" means it comes from the constructed set and standard textbooks alone.
### Insurance archetypes
**LH2. Product rule. Bank only.** *Shortcut engine:* other number = HCF x LCM / known number; with a difference, a x b = LCM / HCF and the difference picks the coprime pair. *Worked example (Sreedhar model test 66):* difference 15, HCF 3, LCM 198: a x b = 66, a - b = 5: 11 and 6, numbers 33 and 18.
**LH3. Ratio with HCF or LCM. Bank only.** *Shortcut engine:* one part = HCF; LCM = a x b x HCF. *Worked example (Sreedhar model test 24):* 3 : 8, LCM 288, HCF 12: numbers 36 and 96.
**LH4. Together again. Unverified.** *Shortcut engine:* LCM of the intervals, added to the start time.
**LH5. Biggest equal piece. Unverified.** *Shortcut engine:* HCF of the lengths; count = sum of length / HCF.
**LH6. Remainders. Unverified.** *Shortcut engine:* the recipes in the grid; always check by dividing.
**LH1. Finding LCM and HCF. Unverified.** *Shortcut engine:* primes, or the division method.
**LH7. Fractions. Unverified.** *Shortcut engine:* the fraction formulas.
## 3. Drill Set
Five original questions. Every answer and elimination hack was checked by script.
### Drill 1. Together again (LH4)
Three bells ring every 9, 12 and 15 minutes. They ring together at 10:00 a.m. When do they next ring together?
1. 10:36 a.m. (B) 11:00 a.m. (C) 1:00 p.m. (D) 12:00 noon (E) 10:45 a.m.
- **Conventional method.** List multiples of 15 until one is divisible by 9 and 12.
- **30-second exam method.** 9 = 3², 12 = 2² x 3, 15 = 3 x 5: LCM = 2² x 3² x 5 = 180 minutes = 3 hours: 1:00 p.m. **Answer (C).**
- **Option elimination hack.** The gap must be a multiple of 15 and of 9 and 12: 36 and 45 minutes fail; 60 fails 9; 120 fails 9; 180 passes.
- **IBPS trap warning.** 10:36 a.m. uses only 9 and 12.
### Drill 2. Biggest piece (LH5)
Three ropes of 1.26 m, 1.98 m and 2.34 m are cut into equal pieces as long as possible. How many pieces are there?
1. 18 (B) 31 (C) 9 (D) 13 (E) 26
- **Conventional method.** HCF of 126, 198, 234 = 18; pieces 7 + 11 + 13.
- **30-second exam method.** Each length is 18 x (7, 11, 13): 31 pieces. **Answer (B).**
- **Option elimination hack.** The piece must divide the gaps between the lengths (72 and 36), so try 18: 126, 198 and 234 all divide by 18. Count 7 + 11 + 13 = 31.
- **IBPS trap warning.** Option (A) 18 is the piece length, not the count.
### Drill 3. Product rule (LH2)
Two numbers have HCF 14 and LCM 420. One of them is 70. Find the other.
1. 84 (B) 42 (C) 98 (D) 140 (E) 56
- **Conventional method.** 14 x 420 / 70.
- **30-second exam method.** 5,880 / 70 = 84. **Answer (A).**
- **Option elimination hack.** The other number must be a multiple of the HCF 14 and divide 420: 84, 42, 140 and 56 qualify; only 84 gives HCF 14 with 70 (70 = 14 x 5, 84 = 14 x 6).
- **IBPS trap warning.** Option (B) 42 gives HCF 14 but LCM 210.
### Drill 4. Remainders (LH6)
Find the smallest number which, divided by 10, 12 and 15, leaves remainders 7, 9 and 12 respectively.
1. 57 (B) 63 (C) 60 (D) 117 (E) 177
- **Conventional method.** Test numbers just under multiples of the LCM.
- **30-second exam method.** Each remainder is 3 short of its divisor; LCM 60 - 3 = 57. **Answer (A).**
- **Option elimination hack.** The answer + 3 must be a multiple of 10, 12 and 15: only 57 + 3 = 60 and 117 + 3 = 120 and 177 + 3 = 180; the smallest is 57.
- **IBPS trap warning.** (D) and (E) also leave those remainders; "smallest" picks 57.
### Drill 5. Ratio (LH3)
Two numbers are in the ratio 5 : 8 and their LCM is 480. Find their HCF.
1. 12 (B) 8 (C) 24 (D) 40 (E) 60
- **Conventional method.** Numbers 5h and 8h; LCM 40h = 480.
- **30-second exam method.** 40h = 480, h = 12. **Answer (A).**
- **Option elimination hack.** 40 x HCF must equal 480: only 12.
- **IBPS trap warning.** (E) 60 is the smaller number.

# Permutations & Combinations - Speed Mastery Module
**Sample.** Papers opened: 23 IBPS Clerk Prelims memory-based papers (2016 to Oct 2025) and the same 12 sister papers. PYQs found: 2 IBPS Clerk Prelims questions from 2 papers (2017 model, Guidely: a committee with one person always included; 2018, Guidely: arrangements of MANAGEMENT); none in the 2019 to 2025 Clerk papers and none in the 12 sister papers. Bank questions used: 131 (129 Guidely in 13 sets, 2 Sreedhar); the clerk bank has none. One Guidely set (letters with repetition, 10 questions) has impossible keys (for example 1,562.5 ways) and is not used. The 8-shift floor is met in papers opened, but the topic is thinly asked. All papers are memory-based reconstructions.
## 1. Core Mental Model, Exact Mechanics & Conversion Grid
**Central law.** Count choices place by place and multiply. Then ask two questions: does order matter (arrange) or not (select)? Is there a rule (fixed place, together, at least, always in)?
### Base operations
1. **And multiplies, or adds.** 9 first digits and 5 second digits: 45 codes.
2. **Arrange all n different things:** n!. With repeats: n! / (a! b! ...).
3. **Arrange r of n:** nPr = n x (n - 1) x ... (r factors).
4. **Select r of n:** nCr = nPr / r!; nCr = nC(n - r).
5. **Together:** tie into a block, arrange units, multiply by arrangements inside.
6. **At least one:** all - none.
7. **Round a table:** (n - 1)!.
### Conversion grid
<table header-row="true">
<tr>
<td>Words in the question</td>
<td>Rule</td>
</tr>
<tr>
<td>words, numbers, codes, seating, ranks</td>
<td>arrange (order matters)</td>
</tr>
<tr>
<td>team, committee, group, handful</td>
<td>select (order does not matter)</td>
</tr>
<tr>
<td>always together</td>
<td>block</td>
</tr>
<tr>
<td>never all together</td>
<td>all - together</td>
</tr>
<tr>
<td>at least one</td>
<td>all - none</td>
</tr>
<tr>
<td>always included / excluded</td>
<td>put in / take out of the pool</td>
</tr>
</table>
### Non-linear warnings
- 5! = 120, 6! = 720, 7! = 5,040, 8! = 40,320, 9! = 362,880, 10! = 3,628,800: the numbers grow fast, so a slip of one factor is never close.
- Selecting counts AB and BA once; arranging counts them twice.

Bank counts are by Guidely set; PN3 (arranging some letters) and PN6 (apart, alternate, round table) sit inside the PN2 and PN5 sets. The 2017 and 2018 questions each come from a single paper, so neither archetype reaches Confirmed.
### Core archetypes (Seen once)
**PN2. Arranging all letters. Seen once.** *Shortcut engine:* n! divided by the factorial of each repeat. *Worked example (IBPS Clerk Prelims 2018, Guidely):* MANAGEMENT has M, A, N, E twice each: 10! / 16 = 226,800.
**PN9. Always included. Seen once.** *Shortcut engine:* put them in and choose the rest from the others. *Worked example (IBPS Clerk Prelims 2017 model, Guidely):* 4 from 7 men and 8 women with one particular woman always in: 14C3 = 364.
### Insurance archetypes (Bank only)
**PN7.** nCr, multiplied across groups. **PN5.** Block method. **PN1.** Place by place, rule first. **PN4.** Fill fixed places first. **PN8.** Add cases, or all minus none.
## 3. Drill Set
Four original questions; every answer was checked by script.
### Drill 1. Repeated letters (PN2)
In how many ways can the letters of COMMITTEE be arranged?
1. 45360 (B) 90720 (C) 362880 (D) 22680 (E) 181440
- **Conventional method.** 9 letters with M, T, E twice each.
- **30-second exam method.** 9! / (2! x 2! x 2!) = 362,880 / 8 = 45,360. **Answer (A).**
- **Option elimination hack.** The answer is 9! divided by a power of 2: (A) is 9!/8, (B) 9!/4, (E) 9!/2; three pairs of repeats means 8.
- **IBPS trap warning.** (B) divides for only two of the three repeated letters.
### Drill 2. Always included (PN9)
A team of 5 is chosen from 11 players with one particular player always included. How many teams?
1. 210 (B) 462 (C) 252 (D) 120 (E) 330
- **Conventional method.** List with and without him; only "with" is allowed.
- **30-second exam method.** Choose 4 more from 10: 10C4 = 210. **Answer (A).**
- **Option elimination hack.** It must be less than 11C5 = 462: rule out (B).
- **IBPS trap warning.** (B) ignores the condition.
### Drill 3. At least one (PN8)
From 6 men and 4 women, a committee of 3 is chosen with at least one woman. How many committees?
1. 100 (B) 120 (C) 96 (D) 80 (E) 116
- **Conventional method.** 1 woman + 2 women + 3 women = 60 + 36 + 4.
- **30-second exam method.** All 10C3 = 120, minus all-men 6C3 = 20: 100. **Answer (A).**
- **Option elimination hack.** Must be 120 minus a selection count: 100 = 120 - 20.
- **IBPS trap warning.** (B) 120 forgets to remove the all-men committees.
### Drill 4. Together (PN5)
In how many ways can the letters of GARDEN be arranged so that the vowels are together?
1. 240 (B) 120 (C) 720 (D) 480 (E) 48
- **Conventional method.** Count all 720 and subtract arrangements with A and E apart (480).
- **30-second exam method.** AE as a block + G, R, D, N: 5! x 2 = 240. **Answer (A).**
- **Option elimination hack.** The answer is a multiple of 2 x 5! = 240.
- **IBPS trap warning.** (B) forgets that the block can be AE or EA.

# Probability - Speed Mastery Module
**Sample.** Papers opened: 23 IBPS Clerk Prelims memory-based papers (2016 to Oct 2025) and the same 12 sister papers. PYQs found: 2 IBPS Clerk Prelims questions from 2 papers (2018, Guidely: two balls from a bag after finding a count; 12 Dec 2021 Set 2, Guidely: two balls of one colour); none in the 2022 to 2025 Clerk papers and none in the 12 sister papers. Bank questions used: 182 (180 Guidely in 17 sets, 2 Sreedhar); the clerk bank has none. All papers are memory-based reconstructions.
## 1. Core Mental Model, Exact Mechanics & Conversion Grid
**Central law.** Probability = favourable outcomes / all outcomes, when each outcome is equally likely. Count both with the rules of Permutations & Combinations.
### Base operations
1. **One item:** count the wanted ones, divide by all.
2. **n coins:** 2ⁿ outcomes; exactly r heads: nCr / 2ⁿ. **Two dice:** 36 outcomes.
3. **Several drawn at once:** selections wanted / nCr.
4. **Or (no overlap):** add. **Or (with overlap):** A + B - both. **Not:** 1 - P.
5. **One after another:** multiply; without replacement, reduce the counts after each draw.
### Conversion grid
<table header-row="true">
<tr>
<td>Words</td>
<td>Rule</td>
</tr>
<tr>
<td>at least one</td>
<td>1 - none</td>
</tr>
<tr>
<td>same colour</td>
<td>add each colour's pairs</td>
</tr>
<tr>
<td>different colours (one of each)</td>
<td>multiply the counts</td>
</tr>
<tr>
<td>either A or B</td>
<td>A + B - both</td>
</tr>
<tr>
<td>with replacement</td>
<td>same chances each draw; multiply</td>
</tr>
<tr>
<td>without replacement</td>
<td>one fewer each draw; multiply</td>
</tr>
</table>
### Two-dice sums
<table header-row="true">
<tr>
<td>Sum</td>
<td>2</td>
<td>3</td>
<td>4</td>
<td>5</td>
<td>6</td>
<td>7</td>
<td>8</td>
<td>9</td>
<td>10</td>
<td>11</td>
<td>12</td>
</tr>
<tr>
<td>Ways</td>
<td>1</td>
<td>2</td>
<td>3</td>
<td>4</td>
<td>5</td>
<td>6</td>
<td>5</td>
<td>4</td>
<td>3</td>
<td>2</td>
<td>1</td>
</tr>
</table>
### Non-linear warnings
- Drawing two at once is not (1/2) x (1/2): the second draw has one fewer.
- "Either" with an overlap double-counts unless the overlap is removed.

### Core archetypes
**PB3. Drawing from a bag. Confirmed (2 papers).** *Shortcut engine:* wanted selections / nCr. *Worked example (IBPS Clerk Prelims 12 Dec 2021 Set 2, Guidely):* 5 red of 10 balls, two drawn, both red: 5C2 / 10C2 = 10/45 = 2/9.
**PB6. A count is missing. Seen once.** *Shortcut engine:* write the given probability with the unknown and match. *Worked example (IBPS Clerk Prelims 2018, Guidely):* 12 blue and 8 yellow or pink; P(yellow) = 1/4, so yellow 5 and pink 3; both pink: 3C2 / 20C2 = 3/190.
### Insurance archetypes (Bank only)
**PB7.** Multiply step by step. **PB1.** Count and divide. **PB2.** 2ⁿ and 36 outcomes. **PB4.** Add the cases. **PB5.** 1 - opposite. **PB8.** A + B - both.
## 3. Drill Set
Four original questions; every answer was computed by script.
### Drill 1. Bag (PB3)
A bag has 4 red and 6 green balls. Two are drawn. What is the probability that both are green?
1. 1/3 (B) 2/5 (C) 3/10 (D) 9/25 (E) 2/15
- **Conventional method.** 6C2 / 10C2.
- **30-second exam method.** 15/45 = 1/3. **Answer (A).**
- **Option elimination hack.** It must be below (6/10)² = 9/25, because the second draw has fewer greens: rules out (D).
- **IBPS trap warning.** (D) treats the draws as with replacement.
### Drill 2. Missing count (PB6)
A bag has 5 red, 3 blue and some white balls. The probability of drawing a white ball is 1/3. Two balls are drawn. What is the probability both are red?
1. 5/33 (B) 1/11 (C) 2/11 (D) 25/144 (E) 1/6
- **Conventional method.** White = 4 (a third of 12); 5C2 / 12C2.
- **30-second exam method.** 10/66 = 5/33. **Answer (A).**
- **Option elimination hack.** It must be below (5/12)² = 25/144 because the second red is drawn from fewer: (A), (B) or (E); 10/66 simplifies to 5/33.
- **IBPS trap warning.** (D) squares 5/12.
### Drill 3. At least one (PB5)
Three coins are tossed. What is the probability of at least one tail?
1. 7/8 (B) 3/8 (C) 1/2 (D) 1/8 (E) 3/4
- **30-second exam method.** 1 - 1/8 = 7/8. **Answer (A).**
- **Option elimination hack.** At least one of three is likely, so above 1/2: (A) or (E).
- **IBPS trap warning.** (B) 3/8 is exactly one tail; (D) 1/8 is the opposite event, no tail at all.
### Drill 4. One after another (PB7)
Two cards are drawn one after the other without replacement. What is the probability both are aces?
1. 1/221 (B) 1/169 (C) 1/13 (D) 2/221 (E) 4/663
- **30-second exam method.** 4/52 x 3/51 = 12/2652 = 1/221. **Answer (A).**
- **Option elimination hack.** Must be below (1/13)² = 1/169: (A), (D) or (E); compute.
- **IBPS trap warning.** (B) assumes replacement.

# Number Series - Speed Mastery Module
**Sample.** Papers opened: 23 IBPS Clerk Prelims memory-based papers (2016 to Oct 2025) and the same 12 sister papers. PYQs found: 70 IBPS Clerk Prelims number series questions from 15 paper labels (in your bank as PYQ-SER-01 to 70; 42 missing-number, 28 wrong-number), and about 24 sister-exam questions from 7 papers (SBI Clerk Feb 2025, IBPS RRB Clerk Dec 2025 three shifts, IBPS PO 2025 and 2026). Bank questions used: 100 clerk questions (30 constructed, 70 PYQ), every one classified by a rule-finding script. The floor is met many times over. All papers are memory-based reconstructions.
## 1. Core Mental Model, Exact Mechanics & Conversion Grid
**Central law.** A series hides one rule. Test in a fixed order and stop at the first that fits: **gaps, gaps of gaps, ratios, multiply-and-add, taking turns**. The size of the growth tells you where to start.
### The test order
1. **Growth under double each step:** write the gaps. Steady gaps or evenly growing gaps settle most of these.
2. **Gaps uneven:** write the gaps of the gaps; or recognise the gaps as squares, cubes, primes or doubling.
3. **Growth x2 or more each step:** write the ratios (next / previous). Ratios 2, 3, 4 ...; 1/7, 1/6, 1/5 ...; 0.5, 1, 1.5 ...
4. **Ratios almost whole:** multiply-and-add. Guess the multiplier, see what is left over: x2 + 1, x1 + 1, x2 + 2, x3 + 3 ...
5. **Zig-zag:** gaps with alternating signs, or two series woven together, or two operations taking turns.
### Recognition grid
<table header-row="true">
<tr>
<td>Gaps or ratios</td>
<td>Rule</td>
</tr>
<tr>
<td>1, 4, 9, 16, 25</td>
<td>squares</td>
</tr>
<tr>
<td>1, 8, 27, 64, 125</td>
<td>cubes</td>
</tr>
<tr>
<td>2, 3, 5, 7, 11, 13</td>
<td>primes</td>
</tr>
<tr>
<td>4, 9, 25, 49, 121</td>
<td>squares of primes</td>
</tr>
<tr>
<td>1, 2, 6, 24, 120</td>
<td>x2, x3, x4 ... on the gaps</td>
</tr>
<tr>
<td>ratios 0.5, 1, 2, 4</td>
<td>the multiplier doubles</td>
</tr>
</table>
### Non-linear warnings
- A wrong number spoils **two** neighbouring gaps; one corrected term must fix both.
- With the blank inside, check the filled value against both neighbours.
## 2. Archetypes

<td>IBPS Clerk PYQs</td>

<td>Bank questions</td>
<td>Group</td>
</tr>
<tr>
<td>NS1</td>
<td>Gaps steady or growing evenly</td>
<td>Confirmed (11 Clerk papers)</td>
<td>20</td>
<td>5</td>
<td>28</td>
<td>Core</td>
</tr>
<tr>

<td>Multiply or divide by changing numbers</td>
<td>Confirmed (11 Clerk papers)</td>
<td>12</td>

<td>15</td>

</tr>
<tr>

<td>Core</td>
</tr>
<tr>

</tr>
<tr>

<td>Confirmed (8 Clerk papers)</td>
<td>9</td>

</tr>
<tr>
<td>NS5</td>
<td>Named families (primes, n² ± 1, each term the sum of two before)</td>
<td>Bank only</td>
<td>0</td>
<td>0</td>
<td>3</td>
<td>Insurance</td>
</tr>
</table>
Sister counts were classified by hand from the paper text, without options; treat them as close, not exact.
### Core archetypes (Confirmed)
**NS1. Gaps. Confirmed.** *Shortcut engine:* gaps, then gaps of gaps. *Worked example (IBPS Clerk Prelims 2016):* 21, 24, 32, 45, 63: gaps 3, 8, 13, 18 grow by 5; next 23, so 86.
**NS4. Multiply or divide. Confirmed.** *Shortcut engine:* ratios. *Worked example (IBPS Clerk Prelims 24 Aug 2024 Shift 1):* 12600, 1800, 300, 60, ?, 5: divide by 7, 6, 5, 4, 3: 15.
**NS2. Gap patterns. Confirmed.** *Shortcut engine:* name the gaps from the recognition grid. *Worked example (IBPS Clerk Prelims 12 Dec 2021 Set 1):* 48, 52, 61, 86, 135: gaps 4, 9, 25, 49 (squares of primes); next 121: 256.
**NS3. Multiply then add. Confirmed.** *Shortcut engine:* guess the multiplier from rough division, the remainder gives the addition. *Worked example (IBPS Clerk Prelims 4 Oct 2025 Shift 3):* 19, 21, 44, 134: x1 + 2, x2 + 2, x3 + 2; next x4 + 2 = 538.
**NS6. Taking turns. Confirmed.** *Shortcut engine:* signed gaps, or split odd and even places. *Worked example (IBPS Clerk Prelims 2024):* 69, 78, 65, 82, 61: +9, -13, +17, -21; next +25 = 86.
### Insurance archetype (Bank only)
**NS5. Named families.** Primes in order; n² + 1 and n² - 1 taking turns; each term the sum of the two before.
## 3. Drill Set
Six original questions, one per Confirmed archetype and the wrong-number form; every answer was checked by the rule-finding script, and the script found exactly one option that fits.
### Drill 1. Easy cutoff-buster (NS1)
17, 20, 26, 35, 47, ?
1. 62 (B) 60 (C) 59 (D) 65 (E) 64
- **Conventional method.** Gaps 3, 6, 9, 12; next 15.
- **30-second exam method.** 47 + 15 = 62. **Answer (A).**
- **Option elimination hack.** Every gap is a multiple of 3, so the answer leaves remainder 2 on division by 3, like 47: 62, 59 and 65 qualify; only 62 continues the gaps.
- **IBPS trap warning.** (B) 60 adds 13 by misreading the step.
### Drill 2. Gap pattern (NS2)
4, 9, 25, 49, 121, ?
1. 144 (B) 169 (C) 196 (D) 225 (E) 256
- **30-second exam method.** The terms are squares of the primes 2, 3, 5, 7, 11; next 13² = 169. **Answer (B).**
- **Option elimination hack.** Only squares of primes qualify: 169.
- **IBPS trap warning.** (A) 144 = 12² continues "squares" without the primes.
### Drill 3. Multiply (NS4)
7, 7, 14, 42, 168, ?
1. 504 (B) 672 (C) 840 (D) 1008 (E) 336
- **30-second exam method.** x1, x2, x3, x4; next x5 = 840. **Answer (C).**
- **Option elimination hack.** The answer is 168 times a whole number above 4: only 840.
- **IBPS trap warning.** (A) 504 repeats x3.
### Drill 4. Multiply then add (NS3)
6, 13, 40, 161, 806, ?
1. 4837 (B) 4842 (C) 4830 (D) 4841 (E) 4846
- **30-second exam method.** x2 + 1, x3 + 1, x4 + 1, x5 + 1; next 806 x 6 + 1 = 4837. **Answer (A).**
- **Option elimination hack.** The answer is one more than a multiple of 806 x 6 = 4836: only 4837.
- **IBPS trap warning.** (C) 4830 drops the +1 and miscounts.
### Drill 5. Taking turns (NS6)
40, 47, 36, 51, 32, ?
1. 53 (B) 55 (C) 57 (D) 49 (E) 51
- **30-second exam method.** +7, -11, +15, -19: sizes grow by 4; next +23: 55. **Answer (B).**
- **Option elimination hack.** The next step is up, and bigger than 19: above 51: (B) or (C).
- **IBPS trap warning.** (E) 51 repeats the last peak.
### Drill 6. Wrong number (NS2)
3, 5, 9, 17, 33, 66, 129
1. 66 (B) 33 (C) 17 (D) 129 (E) 9
- **30-second exam method.** Gaps 2, 4, 8, 16 double; next 32 gives 65, not 66; then 65 + 64 = 129 fits. **Answer (A).**
- **Option elimination hack.** One fix must repair two gaps: changing 66 to 65 makes 32 and 64.
- **IBPS trap warning.** (D) 129 looks odd but is correct.

**Fallback.** If nothing fits after the five tests, split odd and even places; then try the gaps of the gaps once more.

## 6. Verification Log
Built 6 Oct 2026.
### What was tested
- **Answers and archetypes.** A rule-finding script tried every family on all 100 clerk questions with each option in place. On 89 questions exactly the keyed option fits a rule; that rule names the archetype. The other 11 were solved by hand (rules the script does not hold: primes as terms, n² ± 1 taking turns, sum of the two before, cubes counting down, squares of primes going down, signed gaps of n(n + 1)); all 11 agree with their keys.
- **Lesson workings.** The working of every practice question is generated from the rule found, and its last line is checked to show the keyed option.
- **Drills.** All 6 were checked by the script, and the script found exactly one fitting option in each.
### What failed and was fixed
- The first script matched "differences are squares" on an empty list for decimal series and misled the count; it now needs at least three gaps.
- Drill 4's first options did not include the answer (4837); they were rewritten.
### What could not be opened or checked
- The sister-paper series were read from the extracted text without their options and sorted by hand.
- Guidely and Sreedhar series sets were not used; the 100 clerk questions already cover every archetype.
### Source links (pages opened)
- The IBPS Clerk paper links are stored with each question in `clerk/questions.json`; the 12 sister papers are listed in the Simple & Compound Interest module.

# Reasoning: Series and Miscellaneous - Speed Mastery Module
**Sample.** Added on request. This is a Reasoning topic, not Quant, and it combines three bank sections into one: alphanumeric / mixed series, alphabet series, and miscellaneous reasoning. Your bank holds no previous-year Reasoning questions, so every statement about what the exam asks comes from papers opened on the web: 12 IBPS Clerk Prelims memory-based papers (4 Oct 2025 Shifts 1 to 4, 5 Oct 2025 Shift 1 in its Hindi version, 24 Aug 2024 Shift 1, and the 2023, 2022, 2021, 2020, 2019 and 2018 papers) giving 59 questions of these kinds, plus the same 12 sister papers as before (4 SBI Clerk, 4 IBPS RRB Clerk, 4 IBPS PO Prelims) giving 65. Bank questions used: 933 practice questions (540 Guidely + 393 Sreedhar). The floor of 8 target-exam shifts and 6 to 10 sister questions is met. Memory-based papers are reconstructions, not official papers.
## 1. Core Mental Model, Mechanics and Conversion Grid
**The one idea: put numbers on the page and let the pencil count, not your head.** Every question here is mechanical. Nobody gets these wrong for lack of logic; they get them wrong by miscounting or misreading one word. So the method is always: mark, number, read off.
**Position rules (learn these five lines; they replace all counting back and forth):**
<table header-row="true">
<tr>
<td>The question says</td>
<td>It means</td>
</tr>
<tr>
<td>nth to the **right** of mth from the **left**</td>
<td>(m + n)th from the left</td>
</tr>
<tr>
<td>nth to the **left** of mth from the **left**</td>
<td>(m − n)th from the left</td>
</tr>
<tr>
<td>nth to the **left** of mth from the **right**</td>
<td>(m + n)th from the right</td>
</tr>
<tr>
<td>nth to the **right** of mth from the **right**</td>
<td>(m − n)th from the right</td>
</tr>
<tr>
<td>mth from the right in a row of N</td>
<td>(N − m + 1)th from the left</td>
</tr>
</table>
One way to remember it: if the movement takes you **away from** the end you are counting from, add. If it takes you **back towards** that end, subtract.
**The four habits:**
1. **Number the row once.** Write 5, 10, 15, 20 under every fifth element as soon as you see a row. All position questions on that row then take seconds.
2. **For “how many such…” questions, hunt the middle element.** If the question wants a digit with a letter before and a symbol after, look only at digits and check both neighbours. Tick each hit.
3. **For “if all symbols are dropped” questions, do not rewrite the row.** Count along it skipping the dropped kind. Rewrite only if three or more questions use the changed row.
4. **For letter questions, write alphabet numbers above the letters.** Use the grid and the anchors E J O T Y = 5 10 15 20 25.
**Conversion grid: alphabet positions**
<table header-row="true">
<tr>
<td>A</td>
<td>B</td>
<td>C</td>
<td>D</td>
<td>E</td>
<td>F</td>
<td>G</td>
<td>H</td>
<td>I</td>
<td>J</td>
<td>K</td>
<td>L</td>
<td>M</td>
</tr>
<tr>
<td>1</td>
<td>2</td>
<td>3</td>
<td>4</td>
<td>5</td>
<td>6</td>
<td>7</td>
<td>8</td>
<td>9</td>
<td>10</td>
<td>11</td>
<td>12</td>
<td>13</td>
</tr>
</table>
<table header-row="true">
<tr>
<td>N</td>
<td>O</td>
<td>P</td>
<td>Q</td>
<td>R</td>
<td>S</td>
<td>T</td>
<td>U</td>
<td>V</td>
<td>W</td>
<td>X</td>
<td>Y</td>
<td>Z</td>
</tr>
<tr>
<td>14</td>
<td>15</td>
<td>16</td>
<td>17</td>
<td>18</td>
<td>19</td>
<td>20</td>
<td>21</td>
<td>22</td>
<td>23</td>
<td>24</td>
<td>25</td>
<td>26</td>
</tr>
</table>
- Vowels: A E I O U (positions 1, 5, 9, 15, 21). Everything else is a consonant.
- Next letter after Z is A; before A is Z, unless the question says otherwise.
- Letter just after each vowel: B F J P V. Letter just before each vowel: Z D H N T. These are all consonants, so “vowels changed to the next letter” never creates a vowel.
- Consonants that become vowels when moved one step forward: D H N T Z. One step back: B F J P V.
**Non-linear warning.** “Pairs of letters with as many letters between them as in the alphabet” must be counted in **both** directions when the question says “both forward and backward”. Counting only left to right gives a smaller, wrong number that is always among the options.
**Universal fallback (works on every question in this topic):** write the row or word out with numbers above it, do exactly what the sentence says one clause at a time, and read the answer off the page. If stuck, do that and nothing else.

The row family splits by what is asked. In the papers: counting by neighbours (R1) 9 Clerk and 8 sister; finding a position (R2) 5 Clerk and 8 sister; an operation first, then count or position (R3) 4 Clerk and 4 sister; a pattern of groups (R4) 2 Clerk and none in sister papers; 4 more Clerk questions (2019) were on a row but their wording did not come through. In the bank: R1 123, R2 74, R3 82, R4 41.
What stands out:
- The pairs question is the most regular item in the sample: 11 of the 12 Clerk papers had exactly one.
- A paper usually carries either a row with 5 questions or a word set with 5 questions, plus two to four single questions (pairs, changed word, word formation, odd one out).
- Sets of 3-digit numbers fill 101 bank questions and appeared in none of the 24 papers.
### Core archetypes
**R1. Row: count by neighbours. Confirmed.** *Pattern clues:* “how many such … each of which is immediately preceded by … and immediately followed by …”. *Shortcut engine:* find every element of the kind asked for, check its left and right neighbour, tick. *Worked example (IBPS Clerk Prelims 4 Oct 2025 Shift 3):* row 3 G 6 @ B K 9 # T \$ 2 E U Q € ★ 5 M F % L 8 R V © 7 W S 4 H A. Symbols preceded by a consonant and followed by a digit: go symbol by symbol. @ has 6 before, no. # has 9 before, no. \$ has T before and 2 after, yes. € has Q before, ★ after, no. ★ has € before, no. % has F before, L after, no. © has V before and 7 after, yes. Answer two.
**R2. Row: find a position. Confirmed.** *Pattern clues:* “which element is nth to the left of mth from the right end”. *Shortcut engine:* turn it into one position with the five-line table, then count once. *Worked example (same paper):* 3rd to the left of the 10th from the right = 13th from the right. Counting from the right end: A, H, 4, S, W, 7, ©, V, R, 8, L, %, F. Answer F.
**R3. Row: operation first. Confirmed.** *Pattern clues:* “if all the symbols are removed”, “if the second half is reversed”. *Shortcut engine:* reduce to one position, then count along the row skipping what was removed. *Worked example (same paper):* remove all symbols; 5th to the right of the 12th from the left = 17th from the left. Counting only letters and digits: 3, G, 6, B, K, 9, T, 2, E, U, Q, 5, M, F, L, 8, R. Answer R.
**R4. Row: pattern of groups. Confirmed (IBPS Clerk Prelims 2021 and 4 Oct 2025 Shift 3).** *Pattern clues:* a line such as “RSD WKC VJE ?” built from the row. *Shortcut engine:* write the row positions of each letter of each group; the positions move by a steady rule from group to group. *Illustration on the 4 Oct 2025 Shift 3 row (my own example, not a paper question):* 3G6, BK9, T\$2, ? The groups sit at positions 1 to 3, 5 to 7, 9 to 11: three taken, one skipped. Next is positions 13 to 15 = U Q €. The 2021 paper’s question of this kind did not come through clearly enough for me to confirm its answer, so it is counted as a sighting only.
**R8. Pairs. Confirmed.** *Pattern clues:* “how many pairs of letters … as many letters between them in the word as in the alphabet”. *Shortcut engine:* write alphabet numbers above the letters. A pair counts when the difference in alphabet numbers equals the difference in their places in the word, whichever of the two comes first. Go letter by letter and look only at later letters. For digits in a number, use the digits themselves. *Worked example (IBPS Clerk Prelims 4 Oct 2025 Shift 2):* BUTTERFLY = 2, 21, 20, 20, 5, 18, 6, 12, 25. U (place 2) and T (place 3): one place apart, values 21 and 20, one apart: yes. T (place 4) and R (place 6): two places apart, values 20 and 18: yes. T (place 4) and Y (place 9): five places apart, values 20 and 25: yes. No other pair fits. Answer three.
**R9. Changed word or number. Confirmed.** *Pattern clues:* “each vowel is changed to the next letter and each consonant to the previous letter”; “digits arranged in ascending order, how many stay in place”. *Shortcut engine:* write the new line directly under the old one, place by place, then count. *Worked example (IBPS RRB Clerk Prelims 13 Dec 2025 Shift 1):* 589421367 in ascending order is 123456789. Place by place: 5 over 1, 8 over 2, 9 over 3, 4 over 4, 2 over 5, 1 over 6, 3 over 7, 6 over 8, 7 over 9. Only the 4th place is the same. Answer one.
**R10. Word formation. Confirmed.** *Pattern clues:* “if a meaningful word is formed using the 2nd, 5th, 8th and 9th letters of …”. *Shortcut engine:* pull the letters out, try the consonant-vowel shapes. Then apply the question’s own rule: usually one word means give its nth letter, no word means X, more than one means Z. Read which letter stands for which case; papers change it. *Worked example (IBPS Clerk Prelims 2019):* TRANSLATION, letters 2, 5, 8, 9 are R, S, T, I. One common word: STIR. The key gives T, its second letter.
**R11. Odd one out. Confirmed.** *Pattern clues:* five groups of three or four letters. *Shortcut engine:* write the gaps between letters for each group; four share a gap pattern, one does not. *Worked example (IBPS Clerk Prelims 2019):* BEH, JMP, TWZ, CFI, KOP. Gaps: +3 +3 for the first four; KOP is +4 +1. Answer KOP.
**R6. Five 3-letter words. Confirmed.** *Pattern clues:* “TAP NOT MAT PQR STB” followed by five questions. *Shortcut engine:* write the five words in a column once. Each question changes them in a different way and always starts again from the original words. *Worked example (IBPS Clerk Prelims 2018):* TAP NOT MAT PQR STB. First and third letters interchanged: PAT, TON, TAM, RQP, BTS. Meaningful: PAT and TON. Answer two.
**R12. Single letter coding. Seen once (IBPS Clerk Prelims 2021).** *Shortcut engine:* line the word up over its code and write the +1 or −1 for each letter; apply the same pattern. *Worked example (that paper):* CIRCLE is DHSBMD: +1, −1, +1, −1, +1, −1. PUBLIC becomes Q, T, C, K, J, B = QTCKJB.
### Insurance archetype (Bank only)
**R7. Five 3-digit numbers.** Same handling as R6: write them in a column, apply the change to each, read off. Typical asks: swap first and last digits then find the largest; add the digits; arrange digits within each number. Start again from the original numbers for every question.
## 3. Drill Set
All nine questions are original, written for this module, and each answer was checked by script. Drills 1 to 4 use one row, as a paper does.
**Row for Drills 1 to 4:** K 7 # R 4 E @ 9 M \$ 2 U T 5 % A 8 W & 3 H 6 \* J
### Drill 1 (Easy, R1)
How many digits in the row are immediately preceded by a letter and immediately followed by a symbol?
1. One (B) Two (C) Three (D) Four (E) None
**Conventional Method:** read the row left to right testing every element.
**30-Second Exam Method:** look only at the nine digits. 7 (K before, # after) yes. 4 (R, E) no. 9 (@ before) no. 2 (\$ before) no. 5 (T, %) yes. 8 (A, W) no. 3 (& before) no. 6 (H, \*) yes. **Answer (C).**
**Option Elimination Hack:** none; a count has to be counted. Back-check: the three hits are K7#, T5%, H6\*.
**IBPS Trap Warning:** “preceded by a letter and followed by a symbol” is not the same as “preceded by a symbol and followed by a letter”. Underline which side is which before you start.
### Drill 2 (R2)
Which element is 5th to the right of the 9th element from the right end?
1. 3 (B) H (C) 6 (D) & (E) W
**Conventional Method:** count 9 from the right end (A), then 5 steps to the right.
**30-Second Exam Method:** right of … from right: subtract. 9 − 5 = 4th from the right. J, \*, 6, H. **Answer (B).**
**Option Elimination Hack:** none needed. Back-check: 9th from the right is A; five steps right of A are 8, W, &, 3, H.
**IBPS Trap Warning:** adding instead of subtracting gives the 14th from the right, which is 2. Moving right while counting from the right end takes you back towards that end, so the number gets smaller.
### Drill 3 (Two-step, R3)
If all the symbols are removed from the row, which element is 4th to the left of the 7th element from the right end?
1. 9 (B) M (C) U (D) 2 (E) E
**Conventional Method:** rewrite the row without symbols (18 elements), then count.
**30-Second Exam Method:** 7 + 4 = 11th from the right. Count from the right skipping symbols: J, 6, H, 3, W, 8, A, 5, T, U, 2. **Answer (D).**
**Option Elimination Hack:** none needed. Back-check: 7th from the right without symbols is A; four to its left are 5, T, U, 2.
**IBPS Trap Warning:** counting the symbols you were told to remove. Put a light stroke through them first if the row is on paper.
### Drill 4 (R4)
What comes in place of the question mark, based on the row? K#R [4@9](mailto:4@9) M2U ?
1. T5% (B) T%A (C) 5%A (D) T%8 (E) U5A
**Conventional Method:** find each group’s elements in the row and compare.
**30-Second Exam Method:** positions: K#R = 1, 3, 4. [4@9](mailto:4@9) = 5, 7, 8. M2U = 9, 11, 12. Each group moves on by four. Next = 13, 15, 16 = T, %, A. **Answer (B).**
**Option Elimination Hack:** the first element moves 1, 5, 9, so the next is the 13th, T. That removes (C) and (E) at once. Back-check: 13th T, 15th %, 16th A.
**IBPS Trap Warning:** taking three in a row (T5%) because the group “looks” consecutive. Each group skips its second neighbour.
### Drill 5 (Product / non-linear trap, R8)
How many pairs of letters are there in the word MONSTER, each of which has as many letters between them in the word (in both forward and backward directions) as they have between them in the English alphabet?
1. Two (B) Three (C) One (D) Four (E) More than four
**Conventional Method:** test all 21 pairs of letters.
**30-Second Exam Method:** M O N S T E R = 13, 15, 14, 19, 20, 5, 18. O and N: places 2 and 3, values 15 and 14: yes (backward). N and R: places 3 and 7, values 14 and 18: yes. S and T: places 4 and 5, values 19 and 20: yes. T and R: places 5 and 7, values 20 and 18: yes (backward). **Answer (D).**
**Option Elimination Hack:** none; count them. Back-check: ON, NR, ST, TR.
**IBPS Trap Warning:** counting forward only gives NR and ST, two, which is option (A). “Both forward and backward” adds ON and TR.
### Drill 6 (R9)
In the word MARKETING, every vowel is changed to the next letter of the alphabet and every consonant to the previous letter. How many vowels are there in the new word?
1. One (B) Two (C) Three (D) None (E) Four
**Conventional Method:** write the new word: L B Q J F S J M F. Count vowels: none.
**30-Second Exam Method:** a vowel moved forward is never a vowel. A consonant moved back becomes a vowel only if it is B, F, J, P or V. MARKETING has none of those. **Answer (D).**
**Option Elimination Hack:** the shortcut above is the hack: check the word for B, F, J, P, V only. Back-check: LBQJFSJMF has no A, E, I, O, U.
**IBPS Trap Warning:** swapping the rule (vowel back, consonant forward). Then D, H, N, T become vowels and the answer changes. IBPS RRB Clerk Prelims 13 Dec 2025 Shift 1 asked this exact form with IMAGINED.
### Drill 7 (Phrasing trap, R10)
If it is possible to make only one meaningful English word with the 2nd, 4th, 6th and 9th letters of the word ORGANISED, the third letter of that word is your answer. If no such word can be made, mark X. If more than one such word can be made, mark Z.
1. A (B) I (C) X (D) R (E) Z
**Conventional Method:** letters are R, A, I, D. Try arrangements: RAID, ARID.
**30-Second Exam Method:** R, A, I, D gives RAID at once; before marking its third letter, spend three seconds looking for a second word: ARID. Two words. **Answer (E).**
**Option Elimination Hack:** none. Back-check: O R G A N I S E D, places 2, 4, 6, 9 are R, A, I, D.
**IBPS Trap Warning:** stopping at the first word and marking its third letter, I, option (B). Always look for a second word. The letters X and Z (or sometimes other letters) are defined in the question itself; read which is which.
### Drill 8 (R11)
Four of the following five are alike in a certain way. Find the odd one out.
1. BDF (B) KMO (C) PRT (D) HJM (E) UWY
**Conventional Method:** convert each to numbers and compare.
**30-Second Exam Method:** gaps: BDF +2 +2, KMO +2 +2, PRT +2 +2, HJM +2 +3, UWY +2 +2. **Answer (D).**
**Option Elimination Hack:** check only the last gap of each once you see the first gaps are all +2. Back-check: J to M skips K and L.
**IBPS Trap Warning:** looking for a meaning (vowels, word shapes) before checking the gaps. In the papers opened, every odd-one-out of this kind was a gap pattern.
### Drill 9 (R6)
TAP BIN GEM SUN ORE. If the letters within each word are arranged in alphabetical order, how many words remain unchanged?
1. None (B) Two (C) One (D) Three (E) Four
**Conventional Method:** APT, BIN, EGM, NSU, EOR. Only BIN is unchanged.
**30-Second Exam Method:** a word is unchanged only if its letters already climb. T-A no. B-I-N yes. G-E no. S-U-N: U to N falls, no. O-R-E: R to E falls, no. **Answer (C).**
**Option Elimination Hack:** none needed. Back-check: B (2), I (9), N (14) climb.
**IBPS Trap Warning:** “arranged within each word” is not “the words arranged in dictionary order”. The second moves whole words; the first moves letters.

</tr>
<tr>

<td>R3</td>

<td>Counting removed elements</td>
</tr>
<tr>
<td>Groups such as K#R [4@9](mailto:4@9) M2U ?</td>
<td>R4</td>
<td>Row positions of each place in the group</td>
<td>Assuming consecutive</td>
</tr>
<tr>
<td>“Pairs of letters … as in the alphabet”</td>
<td>R8</td>
<td>Alphabet numbers above; place gap = value gap</td>

</tr>
<tr>
<td>“Each vowel changed to …, each consonant to …”</td>
<td>R9</td>
<td>New line under old line</td>
<td>Rule swapped; B F J P V and D H N T Z</td>
</tr>
<tr>
<td>“Arranged in ascending order, how many unchanged”</td>
<td>R9</td>
<td>Sorted line under the original, compare place by place</td>
<td>Ascending vs descending</td>
</tr>
<tr>
<td>“Meaningful word from the 2nd, 5th … letters”</td>
<td>R10</td>
<td>Pull letters, find one word, then look for a second</td>
<td>X and Z cases</td>
</tr>
<tr>
<td>Five letter groups, odd one out</td>
<td>R11</td>
<td>Gaps between letters</td>
<td>Hunting for meaning</td>
</tr>
<tr>
<td>Five 3-letter words</td>
<td>R6</td>
<td>Column of words; each question restarts from the original</td>
<td>Within-word vs between-word ordering</td>
</tr>
</table>
**Five facts to carry in:**
1. E J O T Y = 5, 10, 15, 20, 25.
2. Away from the counting end add; towards it subtract.
3. A vowel moved one step forward is never a vowel.
4. Consonants that turn into vowels: one step back B F J P V; one step forward D H N T Z.
5. Dictionary order: compare first letters, then second, then third.

Links: the 4 Oct 2025 Shift 1, 5 Oct 2025 Shift 1 and 24 Aug 2024 PDFs are listed in the Ages log; the others are on Adda247’s IBPS Clerk previous-year page (adda247.com/jobs/ibps-clerk-previous-year-question-paper). The 12 sister papers are the same PDFs as in the Simple & Compound Interest log.
**What I recomputed from the papers, and what I found.** I recomputed 38 paper questions by script (all the pairs questions, the unchanged-position questions, and 13 row questions). 29 agree with the answer as extracted, 8 do not, and 1 answer did not come through. The 8 are: pairs in MONETARY, 38172946, OBLIQUE, DECLARING and COUNTER; unchanged positions in HUNGARILY; one row question each in the SBI Clerk 21 Sep 2025 and IBPS Clerk 4 Oct 2025 Shift 1 papers. I cannot tell whether the word or row was reconstructed wrongly, the printed key is wrong, or the extraction misread it. So the papers are used here only for *which kinds of question appear*, which does not depend on the keys. The remaining 86 paper questions were sorted by type but not re-solved.
**Bank check (933 questions).** Each question was solved independently from its text by a helper run and compared with the key; the helpers used small scripts for the counting and rearranging.
- 900 agree with the key.
- 20 are ambiguous: the key is reached only on one of two fair readings, or depends on whether a rare word (for example DOGE, BUR, WEN, CERT) counts as meaningful.
- 7 keys look wrong: S-MT76-95 (CONFUSED has four pairs, keyed more than four), S-MT67-82, S-MT21-68 (options garbled), S-MT15-91, S-MT12-86, S-MT8-97 (two letters transposed in the keyed code), and Guidely three-letter set 4 question 16.
- 6 could not be solved because the row or word set is missing from the record (four in Guidely mixed-series set 4, one in three-letter set 2, and S-MT13-90, which turns on a doubtful word).
**Limits of that check.**
- One helper run (75 questions, all Guidely 3-digit number sets) worked by hand instead of by script. Those are all in the Bank-only archetype R7.
- Word-formation and “meaningful word” questions rest on judgement of English words, not on a word list.
- In many Guidely sets the row is split between fields or missing for later questions; the helpers rebuilt it from neighbouring questions, and where the order was unclear they used the keys to confirm it. Those questions are counted as agreeing but were not solved blind.
- I did not re-read the 933 individual results; I merged and counted them.
**Drills.** All nine answers were computed by script. The two meaningful-word calls (RAID and ARID in Drill 7; PAT and TON in the 2018 worked example) are my judgement.
**Repeats:** 16 bank questions are exact repeats of another bank question.
**Exceptions to the methods:** none; the methods are procedures for counting. Where a paper’s wording is loose (for example “which symbol is fifth to the right” meaning the fifth symbol, not the fifth element), the options settle it: if the element you land on is not a symbol, re-read.
**Not verified:** the paper keys (see above); how often each kind appears beyond these 24 papers; the 5 Oct 2025 paper was read in Hindi and only its question types were taken from it. Memory-based papers are reconstructions.
**Corrections:** none so far.

## Module 1: Floor and Flat Puzzles - Speed Mastery Module
**Sample.** This module is built mainly from your question bank, as you asked. Bank: 108 puzzle sets with 530 questions (47 floor sets with one person per floor, 61 floor-with-flat sets; 69 sets from Guidely, 39 from Sreedhar). Your bank has no previous-year Reasoning questions, so what the exam asks comes from 24 memory-based papers opened on the web: 12 IBPS Clerk Prelims papers (2018 to Oct 2025) and 12 sister papers (SBI Clerk, IBPS RRB Clerk, IBPS PO Prelims). In the 12 Clerk papers there were 2 floor sets and 3 floor-with-flat sets; in the 12 sister papers, 3 and 3. Memory-based papers are reconstructions, not official papers.
**A note on time.** A puzzle set is one arrangement followed by about five questions. Nobody solves the arrangement in 45 seconds. The honest target is 4 to 5 minutes for the arrangement and 15 to 20 seconds for each question after it, which works out to about a minute per mark.
### 1. Basic Type, Core Mental Model and Grid
**What the basic type looks like.** A building has numbered floors, 1 at the bottom. In a **floor** puzzle one person lives on each floor. In a **floor-with-flat** puzzle each floor has two flats side by side (Flat A to the west, Flat B to the east; some papers call them P and Q or 1 and 2), and one person lives in each flat. Clues tell you who is above, below or beside whom. You have to rebuild the whole building.
**The one idea: draw the building first, then place the most fixed clue, and split into cases only when you must.**
**How to draw it**
- Floor puzzle: one column of boxes, floor numbers written on the left, highest at the top.
- Floor-with-flat: two columns, West (Flat A) on the left and East (Flat B) on the right, floor numbers on the left. It is a small map: up is north, right is east.
**The solving algorithm (use it on every set):**
1. Draw the grid. Write the names beside it and strike each one as you place it.
2. Sort the clues in your head into three kinds: **fixed** (“C lives on floor 4”, “only two persons live below M”), **linked** (“only two floors between K and N”, “A lives immediately above E”) and **loose** (“D lives above F”, “B is on an even floor”).
3. Place a fixed clue first. If there is none, start with the linked clue that has the biggest gap, because it fits in the fewest places.
4. A linked clue attached to something already placed gives one or two positions. If two, draw the grid twice side by side (Case 1, Case 2).
5. Use the remaining linked clues to kill cases. A case dies when a person needs a floor that does not exist or is already taken.
6. Use loose clues last. They usually settle the final two or three people.
7. Answer the questions from the finished grid.
**Grid: what the words mean**
<table header-row="true">
<tr>
<td>Wording</td>
<td>Meaning</td>
<td>Common mistake</td>
</tr>
<tr>
<td>X lives **immediately** above Y</td>
<td>X is on the very next floor up</td>
<td>none</td>
</tr>
<tr>
<td>X lives above Y</td>
<td>X is on any higher floor</td>
<td>Treating it as the next floor</td>
</tr>
<tr>
<td>Only two **floors** between X and Y</td>
<td>Floor numbers differ by 3</td>
<td>Making them differ by 2</td>
</tr>
<tr>
<td>Only two **persons** live between X and Y</td>
<td>Two people in between; a vacant floor is not counted</td>
<td>Counting the vacant floor</td>
</tr>
<tr>
<td>Only two persons live below X</td>
<td>X is the 3rd person from the bottom</td>
<td>Putting X on floor 2</td>
</tr>
<tr>
<td>As many live above X as below Y</td>
<td>Count from the top for X, from the bottom for Y, same number</td>
<td>Using the same end for both</td>
</tr>
<tr>
<td>X lives to the west / east of Y</td>
<td>Same floor, other flat</td>
<td>Putting X on another floor</td>
</tr>
<tr>
<td>X lives to the north / south of Y</td>
<td>Same flat, a higher / lower floor</td>
<td>Changing the flat</td>
</tr>
<tr>
<td>X lives to the north-east of Y</td>
<td>X in the east flat, Y in the west flat, X on a higher floor (any higher floor, unless it says immediately)</td>
<td>Using only the next floor up</td>
</tr>
<tr>
<td>X lives immediately below Y (flat puzzle)</td>
<td>Usually the next floor down in the same flat; some bank sets mean the next floor in either flat</td>
<td>Not trying the second meaning when the first fails</td>
</tr>
</table>
**Two free facts in every flat puzzle.** If someone lives to the west of X, then X is in the east flat (Flat B). If someone lives to the north-east of X, then X is in the west flat and is not on the top floor.
**Non-linear warning.** “Two floors between” and “two floors above” are different: the first is a difference of 3, the second a difference of 2. Write the difference as a number beside the clue before placing it.
**Universal fallback (works on every set in this topic):** draw the grid once for each possible position of the most restricted person and test every clue in each grid. It is slower but it cannot fail.
### 2. Archetypes
Bank counts are sets out of 108. Paper counts are sets seen in the 12 IBPS Clerk Prelims papers and in the 12 sister papers.

F3 and F4 are counted inside F1 and F2 as well; they are features, not separate buildings.
Where they were seen:
- Floors only: IBPS Clerk 4 Oct 2025 Shift 3 and 5 Oct 2025 Shift 1; SBI Clerk 20 Sep and 21 Sep 2025 Shift 1; IBPS PO 22 Aug 2026 Shift 2 (6 persons, with a dessert each).
- Floor with flat: IBPS Clerk 4 Oct 2025 Shifts 2 and 3, and 2021; IBPS RRB Clerk 13 Dec 2025 Shift 2 (6 persons); IBPS PO 23 Aug 2025 Shift 2 and 24 Aug 2025 Shift 1.
- Every set in the Clerk papers had 8 persons and 5 questions.
What stands out: in the Clerk papers the plain forms are what appeared. No Clerk set had a vacant floor or a second fact per person. Practise those two after the plain forms are comfortable.
What the bank sets look like (from the 40 sets solved by script): 4 floors × 2 flats is the most common shape (12 of 40), then 8 floors with one person each (9), 7 floors (5), 3 floors × 2 flats (4) and 5 floors × 2 flats (3). In 32 of the 40, a good first clue leaves two cases; in 3 it leaves one; in 5 it leaves three.
#### Core archetypes
**F1. Floors only. Confirmed.** *Pattern clues:* “eight persons live on eight different floors”, “lowermost floor is numbered 1”. *Shortcut engine:* one column. Fixed clue first; biggest-gap linked clue next; two cases at most; loose clues last. *Worked example:* Drill 1 below.
**F2. Floor with two flats. Confirmed.** *Pattern clues:* “each floor has two flats”, “Flat A is to the west of Flat B”, compass words. *Shortcut engine:* two columns. Turn every compass word into (which column, higher or lower). Use the two free facts to fix columns before floors. A pair on the same floor (“U is to the east of P”) needs a whole empty floor, which is often the strongest clue in the set. *Worked example:* Drill 2 below.
**F3. With a second fact per person. Seen once (IBPS PO Prelims 22 Aug 2026 Shift 2).** *Pattern clues:* “each person likes a different colour”. *Shortcut engine:* add one more column to the grid for the second fact. Solve the positions first using the clues that mention only people; then drop the facts in. A clue such as “the one who likes red lives immediately above K” places a fact on a floor even before you know who lives there.
#### Insurance archetype (Bank only)
**F4. With a vacant floor or flat.** *Shortcut engine:* treat VACANT as one more name to place. Then re-read every counting clue: “floors between” counts the vacant floor, “persons between” does not. *Worked example:* Drill 3 below.
### 3. Drill Set
Three original puzzles written for this module: basic, exam level, and one built around a wording trap. Each was checked by a brute-force script and has exactly one arrangement.
#### Drill 1 (Basic, F1: floors only)
Six persons A, B, C, D, E and F live on six different floors of a six-storey building. The lowermost floor is numbered 1 and the topmost floor is numbered 6. Only one person lives on each floor.
1. C lives on floor number 4.
2. Only one person lives between C and E.
3. A lives immediately above E.
4. B lives on an even-numbered floor.
5. D lives on one of the floors above F.
**Q1.** Who lives on the topmost floor? (A) D (B) B (C) A (D) C (E) E
**Q2.** How many persons live between B and E? (A) One (B) Two (C) Four (D) Three (E) None
**Q3.** Who lives immediately below D? (A) C (B) A (C) B (D) F (E) E
**Conventional Method:** try each person on each floor and test all five clues until one arrangement survives.
**Fast Method:**
1. Clue 1 is the only fixed clue: C on 4.
2. Clue 2 gives two cases: E on 2, or E on 6.
3. Clue 3: A is just above E. With E on 6 there is no floor above, so that case dies. E on 2, A on 3.
4. Clue 4: free floors are 1, 5, 6. The only even one is 6, so B on 6.
5. Clue 5: D and F take 5 and 1, D above F. D on 5, F on 1.
Arrangement, top to bottom: B, D, C, A, E, F.
**Answers:** Q1 (B). Q2 (D) Three. Q3 (A) C.
**Option Elimination Hack:** for Q1, clue 4 alone says B is on 2, 4 or 6 and clue 1 gives 4 to C; once E takes 2, B must be on 6. You can answer Q1 before finishing the grid.
**IBPS Trap Warning:** clue 5 says “one of the floors above”, not “immediately above”. D is four floors above F. Reading it as immediately above leaves no two neighbouring free floors and makes the puzzle look broken.
#### Drill 2 (Exam level, F2: floor with flat)
Eight persons P, Q, R, S, T, U, V and W live in a four-storey building. The lowermost floor is numbered 1 and the topmost floor is numbered 4. Each floor has two flats, Flat A and Flat B. Flat A is to the west of Flat B. Flat A of floor 2 is immediately above Flat A of floor 1, Flat B of floor 2 is immediately above Flat B of floor 1 and so on. Only one person lives in each flat.
1. R lives on an odd-numbered floor.
2. T lives to the north-west of R.
3. U lives to the east of P on the same floor.
4. V lives immediately below U in the same flat.
5. S lives to the south-west of R.
6. Q lives on an even-numbered floor.
**Q1.** Who lives immediately above W in the same flat? (A) Q (B) R (C) T (D) P (E) S
**Q2.** How many floors are there between Q and V? (A) None (B) One (C) Three (D) Cannot be determined (E) Two
**Q3.** In which floor and flat does S live? (A) Floor 1, Flat A (B) Floor 2, Flat A (C) Floor 1, Flat B (D) Floor 3, Flat A (E) Floor 2, Flat B
**Conventional Method:** list all positions for R, then for each try T and S, and so on through every clue.
**Fast Method:**
1. Clues 2 and 5 both put someone to the west of R, so R is in Flat B. Clue 1: floor 1 or 3.
2. Clue 5 needs a floor below R, so floor 1 dies. R is in Flat B of floor 3.
3. Clue 2: T is above R in Flat A, so T is in Flat A of floor 4. Clue 5: S is in Flat A of floor 1 or 2.
4. Clues 3 and 4: P and U need a whole empty floor, with a free Flat B just below U. Only floors 1 and 2 are fully free, so P and U take floor 2 (P in A, U in B) and V goes to Flat B of floor 1. S therefore goes to Flat A of floor 1.
5. Clue 6: the free flats are Flat A of floor 3 and Flat B of floor 4. Q needs an even floor: Flat B of floor 4. W takes Flat A of floor 3.
Arrangement: Floor 4: T, Q. Floor 3: W, R. Floor 2: P, U. Floor 1: S, V (Flat A first).
**Answers:** Q1 (C) T. Q2 (E) Two. Q3 (A).
**Option Elimination Hack:** for Q3, S is to the south-west of R, so S is in Flat A. That removes (C) and (E) before you solve anything, and (D) goes because S is below R.
**IBPS Trap Warning:** “south-west” does not have to mean the very next floor down. Putting S in Flat A of floor 2 leaves no empty floor for P and U. In your bank, several sets only have a solution when north-east and south-west are read this wider way.
#### Drill 3 (Trap, F4: vacant floor)
Seven persons H, J, K, L, M, N and O live on different floors of an eight-storey building. The lowermost floor is numbered 1 and the topmost floor is numbered 8. Only one person lives on each floor and exactly one floor is vacant.
1. K lives on floor number 5.
2. Only two floors are there between K and N.
3. As many persons live above J as live below N.
4. H lives immediately below O.
5. Only two floors are there between H and the vacant floor.
6. M lives on one of the floors above K.
7. Only one person lives between J and O.
**Q1.** Which of the following floors is vacant? (A) Floor 1 (B) Floor 6 (C) Floor 4 (D) Floor 7 (E) Floor 8
**Q2.** How many persons live between M and H? (A) Two (B) Four (C) Five (D) Three (E) One
**Q3.** Who lives immediately above K? (A) M (B) J (C) O (D) N (E) No one, the floor is vacant
**Conventional Method:** treat the vacant floor as an eighth name and test arrangements against all seven clues.
**Fast Method:**
1. Clue 1: K on 5. Clue 2 (difference of 3): N on 2 or on 8.
2. N on 8: six persons are below N, so by clue 3 six are above J, making J the lowest person; clue 7 then makes O the third person from the bottom. Checking clue 5 against that leaves no place for the vacant floor. This case dies.
3. N on 2: if floor 1 were vacant, clue 5 would put H on 4 and O on 5, which is K’s floor. So floor 1 is occupied, one person is below N, and one person is above J.
4. Clue 6 puts M above K. J has exactly one person above it, so M and J are the top two persons and one of floors 6 to 8 is vacant. K is the third person from the top; clue 7 makes O the fourth: O on 4, H on 3.
5. Clue 5: the vacant floor is three floors from H: floor 6. So M on 8, J on 7, and L on 1.
Arrangement, top to bottom: M, J, vacant, K, O, H, N, L.
**Answers:** Q1 (B). Q2 (D) Three. Q3 (E).
**Option Elimination Hack:** for Q1, clue 5 means the vacant floor is exactly three floors from H, and H sits just under O; once K is on 5, floors 4 and 5 cannot be vacant. That removes (C).
**IBPS Trap Warning:** a vacant floor is a floor but not a person. Between M (8) and H (3) there are four floors but only three persons; “Four” is option (B). Between J and O there are two floors but one person, which is what clue 7 counts.
### 4. Cheat Sheet and Recall Matrix
<table header-row="true">
<tr>
<td>If you see…</td>
<td>Do this</td>
<td>Watch for</td>
</tr>
<tr>
<td>“X lives on floor n”, “only n persons below X”</td>
<td>Place it first</td>
<td>“Two below” means third from bottom</td>
</tr>
<tr>
<td>“Only n floors between X and Y”</td>
<td>Difference = n + 1; one or two cases</td>
<td>Difference of n</td>
</tr>
<tr>
<td>“Immediately above / below”</td>
<td>Glue the two as a block</td>
<td>Top or bottom floor kills a case</td>
</tr>
<tr>
<td>“As many above X as below Y”</td>
<td>Count X from the top, Y from the bottom</td>
<td>Same end for both</td>
</tr>
<tr>
<td>Odd / even floor, “not on floor n”</td>
<td>Keep for the end; it picks between the last candidates</td>
<td>Using it too early</td>
</tr>
<tr>
<td>“West / east of”</td>
<td>Same floor, fixes the column of both</td>
<td>Changing the floor</td>
</tr>
<tr>
<td>“North-east / south-west of”</td>
<td>Fixes both columns; any higher or lower floor</td>
<td>Next floor only</td>
</tr>
<tr>
<td>Two people on the same floor</td>
<td>They need a whole empty floor</td>
<td>Forgetting this strong clue</td>
</tr>
<tr>
<td>“Vacant”</td>
<td>Place VACANT like a person</td>
<td>Floors between vs persons between</td>
</tr>
</table>
**Order of work, in one line:** grid, fixed clue, biggest gap, two cases, kill one, loose clues, questions.
**When a set refuses to work:** re-read the three wordings that most often decide it in your bank: “immediately below” in a flat puzzle (same flat, or just the next floor), compass words (next floor, or any floor), and “persons between” when a floor is vacant.

### 6. Verification Log
**Built:** 6 Oct 2026.
**What was checked by script**
- 40 of the 108 bank sets (20 Sreedhar, 20 Guidely, spread across the sub-sections) were solved by brute force: a script tried every arrangement against every clue as worded, and the questions were then answered from what survived. The other 68 sets were sorted by section name and passage wording but not solved.
- 38 of the 40 have exactly one arrangement. For 26 of those the solver had to note a reading choice; the common ones are listed in the cheat sheet.
- 2 do not: FF120 (the ages of two persons can be swapped) and FF430 (one clue has slipped out of the passage into a question; with it restored the set is unique).
- 197 questions in those sets: 194 agree with the key, 3 do not (S-MT67-93, S-MT67-95, S-MT41-67).
- The three drill puzzles each have exactly one arrangement by script, and their answers come from the script.
**Where the paper evidence comes from.** One helper run opened the 24 PDFs once each and listed the puzzle sets in each Reasoning section. I did not re-open them to cross-check, so a set may have been missed in a paper, and the 5 Oct 2025 paper was read in a Hindi version with partly garbled text. The counts are sightings, not frequencies.
**How the bank check was run.** The 40 sets were solved in helper runs, each writing its own solver script per puzzle. I merged and counted their results; I did not re-read each solver. The “cases” figures are the helpers’ estimate of a human path, not something computed.
**Data problems in the bank.** Several Guidely passages have lost their opening line (the list of persons or the number of floors), and a few carry part of the passage inside a question. Those sets were solved on the assumption that best fits, which the log above counts as a reading choice.
**Not verified:** the 68 unsolved bank sets; the paper inventory beyond a single reading; how often each form appears beyond these 24 papers. Memory-based papers are reconstructions.
**Corrections:** none so far.
## Module 2: Circular and Square Seating - Speed Mastery Module
**Sample.** This module is built mainly from your question bank, as you asked. Bank: 151 seating sets with 715 questions (97 circular sets, 54 square-table sets; 108 sets from Guidely, 43 from Sreedhar). Your bank has no previous-year Reasoning questions, so what the exam asks comes from 24 memory-based papers opened on the web: 12 IBPS Clerk Prelims papers (2018 to Oct 2025) and 12 sister papers (SBI Clerk, IBPS RRB Clerk, IBPS PO Prelims). In the 12 Clerk papers there were 5 circular sets and 5 square-table sets; in the 12 sister papers, 2 and 5. Memory-based papers are reconstructions, not official papers.
**A note on time.** A seating set is one arrangement followed by about five questions. The honest target is 4 to 5 minutes for the arrangement and 15 to 20 seconds for each question after it.
### 1. Basic Type, Core Mental Model and Grid
**What the basic type looks like.** Six to eight people sit around a round table, or around a square table with four at the corners and four at the middle of the sides. Clues say who is to the left or right of whom, who is opposite whom, and who is or is not a neighbour. You rebuild the seating.
**The one idea: a round table has no first seat, so you are free to put the first person anywhere. Put them at the bottom of your drawing and never move them.**
**How to draw it**
- Circle: draw a circle with 8 short dashes (or 6, or 7). Number nothing.
- Square: draw a square. Four seats at the corners, four at the middle of the sides. Going round, corners and middles alternate.
**Left and right: the only rule that matters**
<table header-row="true">
<tr>
<td>The person faces</td>
<td>Their left is</td>
<td>Their right is</td>
</tr>
<tr>
<td>The centre</td>
<td>Clockwise</td>
<td>Anticlockwise</td>
</tr>
<tr>
<td>Outside</td>
<td>Anticlockwise</td>
<td>Clockwise</td>
</tr>
</table>
Left and right always belong to the person named **after** the word “of”. “C sits to the immediate right of A” uses A’s right.
**The solving algorithm (use it on every set):**
1. Draw the table. If facing directions are given, write IN or OUT beside each name before anything else.
2. Pick the person who appears in the most clues and seat them at the bottom.
3. Use the clues measured from that person first (“second to the right of Q”, “third to the left of T”).
4. A clue such as “only one person sits between A and F” gives two seats: draw the table twice.
5. Kill a case with “immediate neighbour” and “not a neighbour” clues.
6. “Opposite” clues need a free pair of facing seats; count how many free pairs are left, often only one.
7. Fill the last seats, then answer.
**Grid: counting round a table of 8**
<table header-row="true">
<tr>
<td>Wording</td>
<td>Seats away</td>
<td>Note</td>
</tr>
<tr>
<td>Immediate left / right</td>
<td>1</td>
<td>Neighbour</td>
</tr>
<tr>
<td>Second to the left / right</td>
<td>2</td>
<td>One person between</td>
</tr>
<tr>
<td>Third to the left / right</td>
<td>3</td>
<td>Two persons between</td>
</tr>
<tr>
<td>Opposite</td>
<td>4</td>
<td>Same from either side</td>
</tr>
<tr>
<td>Third to the left = fifth to the right</td>
<td>3 one way, 5 the other</td>
<td>The two always add up to 8</td>
</tr>
<tr>
<td>Only two persons between X and Y</td>
<td>3 one way (or 5 the other)</td>
<td>Two possible seats</td>
</tr>
</table>
**Square-table facts**
- Moving 2 or 4 seats keeps the seat type: a corner stays a corner, a middle stays a middle. Moving 1 or 3 seats changes it.
- So “N sits in the middle of a side” and “N is second to the right of Q” together tell you Q is also in a middle.
- The neighbours of a corner person are both middle persons, and the other way round.
- A common exam form: corner persons face the centre and middle persons face outside, or the reverse. Then seat type decides left and right.
**Non-linear warning.** For a person facing outside, everything flips. If you solve a mixed-facing table as if all face the centre, you do not get a slightly wrong answer; you get a different arrangement or none at all.
**Universal fallback (works on every set in this topic):** fix one person; for the next most connected person try each remaining seat in turn and run every clue. With 8 seats that is at most 7 small trials.

Where they were seen:
- Circle: IBPS Clerk 4 Oct 2025 Shifts 1 and 4, 24 Aug 2024 Shift 1, 2023 (8 persons each) and 2021 (7 persons); IBPS RRB Clerk 6 Dec 2025 Shift 2 (7 persons); IBPS PO 22 Aug 2026 Shift 2 (7 persons, mixed facing).
- Square table: IBPS Clerk 4 Oct 2025 Shift 3, 5 Oct 2025 Shift 1, 2022, 2019 and 2018; SBI Clerk 21 Sep 2025 and 22 Feb 2025; IBPS RRB Clerk 6 Dec 2025 Shift 1 and 13 Dec 2025 Shifts 1 and 2. All with 8 persons.
- Of the 12 Clerk papers, 10 had a circle or a square set, and none had both.
The paper inventory did not record which way the square-table persons faced in each paper, so I cannot say from the papers whether the “corners in, middles out” form is the usual one. In the 14 square sets from your bank that were solved by script, 9 had corners and middles facing opposite ways, 3 had everyone at corners and middles facing the centre, and 2 had two persons on each side with no one at a corner.
What the bank sets look like (from the 40 sets solved by script): after seating the first person, 36 sets open two cases, 3 open one and 1 opens three.
#### Core archetypes
**C1. Circle, all facing the centre. Confirmed.** *Pattern clues:* “sitting around a circular table facing the centre”. *Shortcut engine:* left is clockwise for everyone. Seat the most-mentioned person, chain the left/right clues, two cases, kill one with a neighbour clue. *Worked example:* Drill 1 below.
**C2. Square table. Confirmed.** *Pattern clues:* “four sit at the corners and four in the middle of the sides”. *Shortcut engine:* use seat type as a free clue (even moves keep it, odd moves flip it). Then solve like a circle of 8. *Worked example:* Drill 2 below.
**C3. Circle, mixed facing. Seen once (IBPS PO Prelims 22 Aug 2026 Shift 2).** *Pattern clues:* “some of them face the centre while others face outside”, or names listed as facing outside. *Shortcut engine:* write IN or OUT beside each name first. For every clue, look at the person after “of” and use that person’s left and right. When facing is not given but must be deduced, clues such as “X and Y face the same direction” or “both neighbours of X face opposite to X” do the deciding. *Worked example:* Drill 3 below.
#### Insurance archetypes (Bank only)
**C4. All facing outside.** Solve it as if all face the centre but swap the words left and right in every clue.
**C5. Unknown number of seats.** Lay the named people out in a line using the gap clues, then use the clue that links the two ends (“as many between A and B as between B and C”) to close the circle and count the seats.
**C6. Vacant seat or second fact.** A vacant seat is a seat but not a person: “persons between” skips it. For a second fact, write it under the name; clues about “the one who likes red” seat a fact before a name.
### 3. Drill Set
Three original puzzles written for this module. Each was checked by a brute-force script and has exactly one arrangement (turning the whole table round does not count as a different one).
#### Drill 1 (Basic, C1: circle facing the centre)
Six friends A, B, C, D, E and F are sitting around a circular table facing the centre, with equal distance between adjacent persons, but not necessarily in the same order. C sits to the immediate right of A. Only one person sits between A and F. F sits to the immediate left of B. B is not an immediate neighbour of A. D is not an immediate neighbour of B.
**Q1.** Who sits opposite to D? (A) A (B) B (C) C (D) E (E) F
**Q2.** How many persons sit between A and B when counted from the left of A? (A) None (B) One (C) Two (D) Three (E) Four
**Q3.** What is the position of F with respect to A? (A) Second to the right (B) Immediate left (C) Second to the left (D) Immediate right (E) Third to the left
**Conventional Method:** fix A and try every order of the other five against the clues.
**Fast Method:**
1. Seat A at the bottom. C is on A’s immediate right (one seat anticlockwise).
2. One person between A and F: F is second to the left of A, or second to the right.
3. F is on B’s immediate left, so B is on F’s immediate right. If F is second to the left of A, B lands next to A, which is not allowed. So F is second to the right of A, and B is opposite A.
4. The two seats on A’s left are for D and E. D must not be next to B, so D is next to A and E is between D and B.
Clockwise from A: A, D, E, B, F, C.
**Answers:** Q1 (E) F. Q2 (C) Two. Q3 (A) Second to the right.
**Option Elimination Hack:** for Q3, C already holds A’s immediate right, so (D) is out; clue 2 says exactly one person is between A and F, so only “second to the left” or “second to the right” can be right.
**IBPS Trap Warning:** “F sits to the immediate left of B” tells you where F is, measured from B. It means B is on F’s right. Reading it the other way round gives a different table.
#### Drill 2 (Exam level, C2: square table)
Eight persons J, K, L, M, N, O, P and Q are sitting around a square table such that four of them sit at the corners and four of them sit in the middle of the sides. All of them are facing the centre, but not necessarily in the same order. N sits second to the right of Q. N sits in the middle of one of the sides. J sits third to the right of Q. L sits opposite to M. M sits to the immediate right of O. L sits second to the right of K.
**Q1.** Who sits third to the left of N? (A) M (B) K (C) P (D) O (E) J
**Q2.** How many persons sit between P and J when counted from the left of P? (A) One (B) Three (C) Four (D) Two (E) None
**Q3.** Four of the following five are alike in a certain way based on their positions in the arrangement and hence form a group. Which one does not belong to that group? (A) J (B) L (C) K (D) M (E) P
**Conventional Method:** fix Q and try every order of the other seven.
**Fast Method:**
1. N is in a middle seat and is two seats from Q, so Q is in a middle seat too. Seat Q at the bottom middle; N is two seats to Q’s right.
2. J is third to the right of Q: the corner just beyond N.
3. L and M are opposite each other. Three of the four facing pairs already hold Q, N or J, so L and M take the seat between N and Q and the corner facing it.
4. M is on O’s immediate right, so O is on M’s immediate left. If M sat between N and Q, O would need Q’s seat. So L is between N and Q, M is opposite, and O is next to M on the J side.
5. L is second to the right of K, so K is second to the left of L: the corner on Q’s left. P takes the last seat.
Clockwise: J (corner), N, L (corner), Q, K (corner), P, M (corner), O.
**Answers:** Q1 (B) K. Q2 (D) Two. Q3 (E) P, the only one not at a corner.
**Option Elimination Hack:** for Q1, N is in a middle seat and three seats away is an odd move, so the answer is a corner person. P and O are middle persons, so (C) and (D) are out.
**IBPS Trap Warning:** “second to the right” is not “immediate right”. Seating N next to Q puts Q on a corner and shifts the whole table by one seat.
#### Drill 3 (Trap, C3: mixed facing)
Eight persons S, T, U, V, W, X, Y and Z are sitting around a circular table with equal distance between adjacent persons. T, V and Y are facing outside (away from the centre) while the remaining five are facing the centre. S sits third to the left of T. Y sits to the immediate left of T. W sits to the immediate left of Y. W sits third to the left of X. Only one person sits between U and W. V is not an immediate neighbour of T.
**Q1.** Who sits second to the right of V? (A) T (B) S (C) U (D) W (E) Z
**Q2.** How many persons sit between Y and Z when counted from the right of Y? (A) One (B) Two (C) Three (D) Five (E) None
**Q3.** What is the position of S with respect to T? (A) Third to the right (B) Second to the left (C) Immediate right (D) Third to the left (E) Second to the right
**Conventional Method:** fix T and try every order of the other seven, taking each person’s own left and right.
**Fast Method:**
1. Mark T, V, Y as OUT: their left is anticlockwise.
2. Seat T. From T: Y is one seat anticlockwise, S is three seats anticlockwise.
3. From Y (OUT): W is one seat anticlockwise of Y, between Y and S.
4. From X (IN): W is three seats clockwise of X, so X is three seats anticlockwise of W. That is the seat opposite Y.
5. U is two seats from W. One such seat is T’s, so U takes the other, between X and S.
6. Two seats are left for V and Z. V is not next to T, so Z is next to T and V is between Z and X.
Clockwise: S, W, Y, T, Z, V, X, U.
**Answers:** Q1 (C) U. Q2 (A) One. Q3 (D) Third to the left.
**Option Elimination Hack:** Q3 is given away by the first clue itself: “S sits third to the left of T”. Check the clues before you look at the drawing.
**IBPS Trap Warning:** for T, V and Y, left is anticlockwise. Treating everyone as facing the centre gives two possible tables instead of one, and Q1 and Q2 come out wrong. V also faces outside, so in Q1 V’s right is clockwise.
### 4. Cheat Sheet and Recall Matrix
<table header-row="true">
<tr>
<td>If you see…</td>
<td>Do this</td>
<td>Watch for</td>
</tr>
<tr>
<td>“Facing the centre”</td>
<td>Left = clockwise</td>
<td>Nothing flips</td>
</tr>
<tr>
<td>“Facing outside”</td>
<td>Left = anticlockwise</td>
<td>Flip every clue</td>
</tr>
<tr>
<td>Mixed facing</td>
<td>IN or OUT beside each name first</td>
<td>Using your own left</td>
</tr>
<tr>
<td>“X is nth to the left of Y”</td>
<td>Count from Y, using Y’s left</td>
<td>Counting from X</td>
</tr>
<tr>
<td>“Only n persons between X and Y”</td>
<td>Two seats, two cases</td>
<td>Forgetting the second seat</td>
</tr>
<tr>
<td>“Opposite”</td>
<td>Needs a free facing pair</td>
<td>A pair already half full</td>
</tr>
<tr>
<td>“Immediate neighbour” / “not a neighbour”</td>
<td>Use to kill cases</td>
<td>Using it to start</td>
</tr>
<tr>
<td>Square table, “sits at a corner / middle”</td>
<td>Even moves keep seat type, odd moves flip it</td>
<td>Ignoring seat type</td>
</tr>
<tr>
<td>“Corners face in, middles face out”</td>
<td>Seat type gives facing</td>
<td>Mixing them up</td>
</tr>
<tr>
<td>Vacant seat</td>
<td>A seat, not a person</td>
<td>Counting it as a person</td>
</tr>
</table>
**Order of work, in one line:** table, IN/OUT marks, most-mentioned person at the bottom, clues from that person, two tables, neighbour clues kill one, opposite pairs, questions.
**Two checks before you answer.** For “who sits nth to the left of X”: is X facing in or out? For “how many persons between X and Y counted from the left of X”: go the way X’s left goes, and do not count X or Y.
