### 6. Verification Log
**Built:** 6 Oct 2026.
**What was checked by script**
- 40 of the 151 bank sets (20 Sreedhar, 20 Guidely, spread across the sub-sections) were solved by brute force: a script tried every seating against every clue as worded, and the questions were answered from what survived. The other 111 sets were sorted by section name but not solved.
- 38 of the 40 have exactly one seating. For 25 of those the solver noted a reading choice, such as “three persons away” meaning three between, “same number between” meaning the shorter way round, or a missing opening line filled in from the options.
- 2 do not: CS618 (an unknown-seat circle whose last clue has slipped into a question; with it the answer is 16 seats) and CS327 (a vacant-seat set that is unique only if the seat between two people must hold a person).
- 205 questions in those sets: 202 agree with the key, 2 do not (S-MT73-75 and S-MT73-77), and 1 cannot be settled because a word is missing from the question (S-MT70-81).
- The three drill puzzles each have exactly one arrangement by script, and their answers come from the script.
**Where the paper evidence comes from.** One helper run opened the 24 PDFs once each and listed the puzzle sets in each Reasoning section. I did not re-open them to cross-check, so a set may have been missed in a paper, and facing directions were not recorded for the square-table sets. The 5 Oct 2025 paper was read in a Hindi version with partly garbled text. The counts are sightings, not frequencies.
**How the bank check was run.** The 40 sets were solved in helper runs, each writing its own solver script per puzzle. I merged and counted their results; I did not re-read each solver. The “cases” figures are the helpers’ estimate of a human path.
**Left out on purpose.** Your bank also has rectangular, triangular, pentagonal and hexagonal table sets (over 100 sets). You named circular and square, and none of those other shapes appeared in the 24 papers, so they are not in this module.
**Not verified:** the 111 unsolved bank sets; the split of circular bank sets by facing direction; the paper inventory beyond a single reading. Memory-based papers are reconstructions.
**Corrections:** none so far.
## Module 3: Month, Date and Day Puzzles - Speed Mastery Module
**Sample.** This module is built mainly from your question bank, as you asked. Bank: 148 puzzle sets with 743 questions (53 month-with-date sets, 43 month sets, 52 day-of-the-week sets; 95 sets from Guidely, 53 from Sreedhar). Your bank has no previous-year Reasoning questions, so what the exam asks comes from 24 memory-based papers opened on the web: 12 IBPS Clerk Prelims papers (2018 to Oct 2025) and 12 sister papers (SBI Clerk, IBPS RRB Clerk, IBPS PO Prelims). In the 12 Clerk papers there were 5 month sets, 1 month-with-date set and 2 day sets; in the 12 sister papers, 2, 4 and 3. Memory-based papers are reconstructions, not official papers.
**A note on time.** A set is one timetable followed by about five questions. The honest target is 4 to 5 minutes for the timetable and 15 to 20 seconds for each question after it.
### 1. Basic Type, Core Mental Model and Grid
**What the basic type looks like.** Seven or eight people each do something (are born, attend a class, have an exam) at a different time. The times are either months, or dates in months (“the 10th and 25th of March, June, September and December”), or days of a week. Clues say who is before or after whom and how many people come between. You rebuild the timetable.
**The one idea: a calendar puzzle is a floor puzzle lying on its side. Write the time slots in a column in calendar order, number them, and it becomes a stack.**
**How to draw it**
- Month puzzle: a column of the months given, earliest at the top. Write 30 or 31 beside each month.
- Month-with-date: two rows per month (for example 10 March, 25 March, 10 June, 25 June…), so 4 months × 2 dates = 8 slots. Mark each slot odd or even date, and each month 30 or 31.
- Day puzzle: a column Monday to Sunday (or Sunday to Saturday if the passage says the week starts on Sunday).
**The solving algorithm (use it on every set):**
1. Write the slots in calendar order and number them 1, 2, 3…
2. Beside the slots, mark what the clues can use: 30 or 31 days, odd or even date.
3. Place a fixed clue first: “born on 20 June”, “only three persons after T”, “in a month having 30 days” (this last one usually leaves two or three slots, which is a good start).
4. Attach linked clues: “only two persons between”, “immediately after”. Each gives one or two slots; draw a second column if two.
5. Kill cases when someone needs a slot that is taken or does not exist.
6. Loose clues (“C was born before F”) last.
**Grid 1: days in each month**
<table header-row="true">
<tr>
<td>31 days</td>
<td>30 days</td>
<td>28 or 29 days</td>
</tr>
<tr>
<td>January, March, May, July, August, October, December</td>
<td>April, June, September, November</td>
<td>February</td>
</tr>
</table>
Knuckle rule: make a fist and count months across the knuckles and the dips between them. Knuckles have 31 days; dips have 30 (February is the short dip). July and August both land on knuckles.
**Grid 2: what the words mean**
<table header-row="true">
<tr>
<td>Wording</td>
<td>Meaning</td>
</tr>
<tr>
<td>X was born immediately after Y</td>
<td>X is in the very next slot</td>
</tr>
<tr>
<td>X was born after Y</td>
<td>Any later slot</td>
</tr>
<tr>
<td>Only two persons between X and Y</td>
<td>Slot numbers differ by 3 (if no slot is empty)</td>
</tr>
<tr>
<td>Only three persons after X</td>
<td>X is the 4th person from the end</td>
</tr>
<tr>
<td>X is three persons after Y; “three persons before”</td>
<td>Slot numbers differ by 3</td>
</tr>
<tr>
<td>As many before X as after Y</td>
<td>X counted from the start equals Y counted from the end</td>
</tr>
<tr>
<td>In a month having 31 days / on an even date</td>
<td>Narrows to the marked slots</td>
</tr>
<tr>
<td>X is older than Y (birth puzzles in one year)</td>
<td>X was born **earlier**</td>
</tr>
<tr>
<td>X is the oldest</td>
<td>X is in the first slot</td>
</tr>
</table>
**Non-linear warning.** In birth puzzles “older” means earlier in the year, so the oldest person is at the top of the column and the youngest at the bottom. Reversing this reverses the whole timetable.
**Universal fallback (works on every set in this topic):** one column for each possible slot of the most restricted person, then run every clue down each column.

Where they were seen:
- Months: IBPS Clerk 4 Oct 2025 Shifts 1, 2 and 4, 24 Aug 2024 Shift 1 and 2019 (8 persons each); IBPS RRB Clerk 6 Dec 2025 Shift 2 (7 persons); IBPS PO 23 Aug 2025 Shift 2 (7 persons, with a colour each).
- Month with date: IBPS Clerk 2023; SBI Clerk 20 Sep 2025 Shift 1 and 22 Feb 2025 (8 persons); IBPS RRB Clerk 6 Dec 2025 Shift 1 (6 persons); IBPS PO 22 Aug 2026 Shift 2 (6 persons, with a cuisine each).
- Days: IBPS Clerk 2023 and 2018; SBI Clerk 20 Sep 2025 Shift 2; IBPS RRB Clerk 13 Dec 2025 Shifts 1 and 2. Seven persons each.
- One Clerk paper (5 Dec 2020) had a year-with-date set, a cousin of D2; years are not covered here.
What stands out: 8 of the 12 Clerk papers had one of these three forms, and the plain month puzzle with 8 persons is the one that keeps coming in Clerk. Month-with-date showed up more in SBI Clerk, RRB Clerk and PO.
A caution on the Clerk “month, 8 persons” sets: the helper that read the papers recorded them as month puzzles. I did not re-open them to check whether some were two dates in four months, which also gives 8 persons. Practise both.
What the bank sets look like (from the 40 sets solved by script): after a good first clue, 20 sets leave two cases and 16 leave three. Expect to carry three columns more often here than in floor or box puzzles.
#### Core archetypes
**D1. Months. Confirmed.** *Pattern clues:* “in seven different months of the same year, viz. January, March, April…”. *Shortcut engine:* column of the months given, 30 or 31 beside each. The “30 days” or “31 days” clue is the usual opener. *Worked example:* Drill 2 below.
**D2. Month with two dates. Confirmed.** *Pattern clues:* “on two different dates, 5th or 20th, of three different months”. *Shortcut engine:* slots in calendar order; mark odd or even date and month length. Two people in the same month sit in neighbouring slots, so “born in the same month as” is a strong clue. *Worked example:* Drill 1 below.
**D3. Days of the week. Confirmed.** *Pattern clues:* “on seven different days of the same week starting from Monday”. *Shortcut engine:* column of seven days. “Only three persons after T” fixes T’s day at once when no day is empty. Check which day the week starts on. *Worked example:* Drill 3 below (with a holiday).
**D4. With a second fact. Seen in IBPS PO only (22 Aug 2026 Shift 2 and 23 Aug 2025 Shift 2).** *Shortcut engine:* a second column beside the names. Solve the order of people first with the clues that mention only people, then fill in the facts.
#### Insurance archetype (Bank only)
**D5. With an empty slot.** Put EMPTY (or HOLIDAY) in as one more name. Clues that count persons skip it; clues that count days or dates do not.
### 3. Drill Set
Three original puzzles written for this module. Each was checked by a brute-force script and has exactly one timetable.
#### Drill 1 (Basic, D2: month with date)
Six persons, Anu, Bina, Charu, Dev, Esha and Farid, were born on two different dates, either the 5th or the 20th, of three different months, March, June and September, of the same year. Only one person was born on each date.
1. Dev was born on 20th June.
2. Only two persons were born between Dev and Anu.
3. Bina was born on an odd-numbered date in a month having 30 days.
4. Esha was born immediately after Bina.
5. Charu was born before Farid.
**Q1.** Who was born on 5th June? (A) Anu (B) Charu (C) Bina (D) Farid (E) Esha
**Q2.** How many persons were born after Charu? (A) One (B) Two (C) Three (D) Four (E) Five
**Q3.** Who was born in the same month as Anu? (A) Farid (B) Charu (C) Dev (D) Bina (E) Esha
**Conventional Method:** try each of the six persons in each of the six slots against the clues.
**Fast Method:**
1. Slots in order: 5 Mar, 20 Mar, 5 Jun, 20 Jun, 5 Sep, 20 Sep. Dev in slot 4.
2. Two persons between Dev and Anu: slot 7 does not exist, so Anu is in slot 1 (5 March).
3. 30-day months here are June and September; odd date is the 5th. Bina is on 5 June or 5 September.
4. Esha is immediately after Bina. After 5 June comes 20 June, which is Dev’s. So Bina 5 September, Esha 20 September.
5. Slots 2 and 3 are left; Charu before Farid: Charu 20 March, Farid 5 June.
Timetable: Anu, Charu, Farid, Dev, Bina, Esha.
**Answers:** Q1 (D) Farid. Q2 (D) Four. Q3 (B) Charu.
**Option Elimination Hack:** for Q2, Charu is in slot 2 of 6, so 6 − 2 = 4 persons come after. Use slot numbers, not the drawing.
**IBPS Trap Warning:** March has 31 days, so it is not a “30-day month”. And “two persons between” is a difference of three slots, not two.
#### Drill 2 (Exam level, D1: months)
Seven persons, G, H, J, K, L, M and N, joined a bank in seven different months of the same year, January, March, April, June, August, October and December, but not necessarily in the same order. Only one person joined in each month.
1. G joined in a month having 30 days.
2. Only one person joined between H and G, and H joined before G.
3. As many persons joined before H as after J.
4. M joined immediately before G.
5. L joined after J.
6. K joined before N.
**Q1.** Who joined in August? (A) K (B) J (C) N (D) L (E) M
**Q2.** How many persons joined between K and J? (A) Four (B) Three (C) Two (D) One (E) Five
**Q3.** In which month did M join? (A) March (B) June (C) January (D) October (E) April
**Conventional Method:** try every order of the seven persons against the six clues.
**Fast Method:**
1. Of the months listed, only April and June have 30 days. G in April (Case 1) or June (Case 2).
2. H is two slots before G with M between them. Case 1: H January, M March, G April. Case 2: H March, M April, G June.
3. Clue 3. Case 1: nobody before H, so nobody after J, so J is in December; then L cannot be after J. Case 1 dies.
4. Case 2: one person before H, so one person after J: J in October, L in December.
5. January and August are left; K before N: K January, N August.
Timetable: K, H, M, G, N, J, L.
**Answers:** Q1 (C) N. Q2 (A) Four. Q3 (E) April.
**Option Elimination Hack:** for Q3, M is immediately before G and G is in April or June, so M is in March or April. Only (A) and (E) can be right.
**IBPS Trap Warning:** August has 31 days. Treating it as a 30-day month opens a third case and wastes a minute.
#### Drill 3 (Trap, D5: days with a holiday)
Six persons, R, S, T, U, V and W, perform in a music show on six different days of the same week, starting from Monday and ending on Sunday. Only one person performs on a day, and one of the days is a holiday on which no one performs.
1. Only three persons perform after T.
2. T performs before the holiday.
3. T performs immediately after W.
4. Only three persons perform between V and W.
5. R performs immediately after U.
6. V performs neither immediately before nor immediately after the holiday.
**Q1.** Which day is the holiday? (A) Wednesday (B) Thursday (C) Friday (D) Saturday (E) Tuesday
**Q2.** How many persons perform between S and U? (A) None (B) One (C) Three (D) Four (E) Two
**Q3.** Who performs on Saturday? (A) R (B) S (C) U (D) V (E) W
**Conventional Method:** treat the holiday as a seventh name and try every order against the six clues.
**Fast Method:**
1. Three persons after T, and the holiday is also after T: four days follow T. T on Wednesday.
2. W is on the day just before T: Tuesday. W is the second performer.
3. Three persons between W and V makes V the sixth and last performer.
4. Thursday to Sunday hold U, R, V and the holiday, with U and R on neighbouring days and V last of the three. Cases: (holiday, U, R, V), (U, R, holiday, V), (U, R, V, holiday).
5. V must not touch the holiday: only the first case survives. Thursday is the holiday; U Friday, R Saturday, V Sunday.
6. S takes Monday.
Timetable: S, W, T, holiday, U, R, V.
**Answers:** Q1 (B) Thursday. Q2 (E) Two. Q3 (A) R.
**Option Elimination Hack:** for Q1, the holiday comes after T, and T has people before it, so Tuesday is out; clue 6 keeps the holiday away from V, who is last, so it cannot be Saturday either.
**IBPS Trap Warning:** the clues count persons, not days. Reading “three persons after T” as “three days after T” puts T on Thursday and the holiday on Friday, which is option (C).

**Order of work, in one line:** slots in order, marks beside them, fixed or month-length clue, linked clues, two or three columns, kill, loose clues, questions.
**Quick facts.** With seven persons on seven days, “three persons after T” means T is on the fourth day. With 8 slots, the pairs that satisfy “as many before X as after Y” add up to 9.

## Module 4: Box Puzzles - Speed Mastery Module
**Sample.** This module is built mainly from your question bank, as you asked. Bank: 77 puzzle sets with 376 questions (57 single-stack sets, 20 sets with two stacks side by side; 53 sets from Guidely, 24 from Sreedhar). Your bank has no previous-year Reasoning questions, so what the exam asks comes from 24 memory-based papers opened on the web: 12 IBPS Clerk Prelims papers (2018 to Oct 2025) and 12 sister papers (SBI Clerk, IBPS RRB Clerk, IBPS PO Prelims). Box sets seen: 3 in the Clerk papers, 4 in the sister papers. Memory-based papers are reconstructions, not official papers.
**A note on time.** A box set is one stack followed by about five questions. The honest target is 3 to 4 minutes for the stack and 15 to 20 seconds for each question after it. Box sets are usually the quickest puzzle in the paper, so do them early.
### 1. Basic Type, Core Mental Model and Grid
**What the basic type looks like.** Six to eight boxes, each with a letter name, are kept one above another. Clues say which box is above or below which, and how many boxes lie between. You rebuild the stack from top to bottom.
**The one idea: a box stack is a floor puzzle with no floor numbers printed. Number it yourself, from the bottom, before reading a single clue.**
**How to draw it.** One column of blank boxes. Write 1 at the bottom and the highest number at the top. Every clue then becomes a number.
**The solving algorithm (use it on every set):**
1. Draw the column and number it from the bottom.
2. Find a fixed clue: “third from the bottom”, “only two boxes below M”, “at the top”. Place it.
3. Attach linked clues to what is placed: “only three boxes between M and K”, “J is immediately above K”. Each gives one or two places.
4. If two places are possible, draw a second column (Case 2). Do not hold cases in your head.
5. Kill a case when a box needs a place that is taken or does not exist.
6. Use loose clues (“U is above P”) last.
**Grid: what the words mean, as numbers**
<table header-row="true">
<tr>
<td>Wording</td>
<td>As a number (stack of N, numbered from bottom)</td>
</tr>
<tr>
<td>Third from the bottom</td>
<td>Place 3</td>
</tr>
<tr>
<td>Third from the top</td>
<td>Place N − 2</td>
</tr>
<tr>
<td>Only two boxes below X</td>
<td>X at place 3</td>
</tr>
<tr>
<td>Only two boxes above X</td>
<td>X at place N − 2</td>
</tr>
<tr>
<td>Only three boxes between X and Y</td>
<td>Places differ by 4</td>
</tr>
<tr>
<td>X is three places above Y; “X is kept three boxes above Y”</td>
<td>Places differ by 3 (two boxes between)</td>
</tr>
<tr>
<td>X immediately above Y</td>
<td>X = Y + 1</td>
</tr>
<tr>
<td>X above Y</td>
<td>X anywhere higher</td>
</tr>
<tr>
<td>As many boxes above X as below Y</td>
<td>(N − place of X) = (place of Y − 1)</td>
</tr>
<tr>
<td>More than two boxes between X and Y</td>
<td>Places differ by at least 4</td>
</tr>
</table>
**Non-linear warning.** “Three boxes between” (difference 4) and “three boxes above” (difference 3) look alike and are not. In your bank, five of the 40 sets checked by script have exactly one solution only when “n boxes above” is read as n places above.
**Universal fallback (works on every set in this topic):** draw one column for each possible place of the most restricted box and run every clue down each column.

Where they were seen: IBPS Clerk 4 Oct 2025 Shifts 1 and 2 and 5 Dec 2020, each with 8 boxes; SBI Clerk 21 Sep 2025 Shift 1 (8 boxes); IBPS RRB Clerk 6 Dec 2025 Shift 2 and 13 Dec 2025 Shift 1 (7 boxes each); IBPS PO 22 Aug 2026 Shift 1 (6 boxes with an item in each).
What stands out: every box set in the Clerk and other clerk-level papers was the plain single stack. The two-stack form takes up a quarter of your bank’s box sets and appeared in none of the 24 papers.
What the bank sets look like (from the 40 sets solved by script): 7 boxes (11 sets) and 8 boxes (10) dominate; 6 boxes (3). After a good first clue, 22 sets leave two cases, 13 leave three, 3 leave four and 2 leave one. So expect one more case than in a floor puzzle, because box sets give fewer fixed clues.
#### Core archetypes
**X1. One stack, names only. Confirmed.** *Pattern clues:* “eight boxes are kept one above another”. *Shortcut engine:* number from the bottom; fixed clue; chain of linked clues; loose clues last. *Worked examples:* Drills 1 and 2 below.
**X2. One stack with a second fact. Seen once (IBPS PO Prelims 22 Aug 2026 Shift 1).** *Pattern clues:* “each box is of a different colour”. *Shortcut engine:* draw two columns side by side, one for the box name and one for the colour, sharing the same place numbers. A clue such as “the red box is immediately below J” fills the colour column even when the name is unknown.
#### Insurance archetypes (Bank only)
**X3. Empty shelf.** Place EMPTY like a box. “Shelves between” counts it; “boxes between” does not.
**X4. Two stacks.** It is a floor-with-flat puzzle: stack X on the west, stack Y on the east, shelves instead of floors. “To the west of” means the same shelf in the other stack. “Above” and “below” in your bank’s sets are sometimes meant within the same stack and sometimes across stacks; if one reading gives no arrangement, try the other.
### 3. Drill Set
Three original puzzles written for this module. Each was checked by a brute-force script and has exactly one arrangement.
#### Drill 1 (Basic, X1)
Six boxes P, Q, R, S, T and U are kept one above another in a stack, but not necessarily in the same order. Box R is kept at the third position from the bottom. Only two boxes are kept between box R and box T. Box S is kept immediately below box T. Only one box is kept between box Q and box R. Box U is kept above box P.
**Q1.** Which box is kept at the bottom of the stack? (A) P (B) S (C) Q (D) U (E) R
**Q2.** How many boxes are kept between box U and box Q? (A) One (B) Two (C) Three (D) Four (E) None
**Q3.** Which box is kept immediately above box R? (A) T (B) P (C) S (D) Q (E) U
**Conventional Method:** try each order of the six boxes against the five clues.
**Fast Method:**
1. R at place 3.
2. Two boxes between R and T: difference 3. Going down is impossible, so T at 6 (top).
3. S immediately below T: S at 5.
4. One box between Q and R: Q at 5 or 1. Place 5 is taken, so Q at 1.
5. Places 2 and 4 are left; U above P: U at 4, P at 2.
Stack, top to bottom: T, S, U, R, P, Q.
**Answers:** Q1 (C). Q2 (B) Two. Q3 (E) U.
**Option Elimination Hack:** for Q1, R is at place 3 so (E) is out at once; T and S end up at the top two places so (B) is out.
**IBPS Trap Warning:** “U is kept above P” is not “immediately above”. U is two places higher than P.
#### Drill 2 (Exam level, X1)
Eight boxes J, K, L, M, N, P, Q and R are kept one above another in a stack, but not necessarily in the same order. Only two boxes are kept below box M. Only three boxes are kept between box M and box K. Box J is kept immediately above box K. Only one box is kept between box J and box N. Only two boxes are kept between box L and box Q, and box L is kept above box Q. Box R is kept below box Q.
**Q1.** Which box is kept immediately below box N? (A) P (B) K (C) M (D) L (E) Q
**Q2.** How many boxes are kept above box P? (A) Four (B) Three (C) Five (D) Two (E) Six
**Q3.** Which box is kept exactly between box K and box L? (A) J (B) N (C) P (D) M (E) R
**Conventional Method:** test orders of the eight boxes against all six clues.
**Fast Method:**
1. Two boxes below M: M at 3.
2. Three boxes between M and K: difference 4, so K at 7.
3. J immediately above K: J at 8. One box between J and N: N at 6.
4. Places 1, 2, 4, 5 are left. L above Q with two between (difference 3): L at 5 and Q at 2, or L at 4 and Q at 1.
5. R is below Q, so Q cannot be at 1. Q at 2, L at 5, R at 1. P takes 4.
Stack, top to bottom: J, K, N, L, P, M, Q, R.
**Answers:** Q1 (D) L. Q2 (A) Four. Q3 (B) N.
**Option Elimination Hack:** for Q2, P is the last box placed and lands at 4 in a stack of 8, so 8 − 4 = 4 boxes above. No need to look at the drawing.
**IBPS Trap Warning:** “only two boxes below M” puts M at place 3, not place 2. Starting one place off shifts K, J and N as well, and every answer changes.
#### Drill 3 (Trap, X1)
Eight boxes S, T, U, V, W, X, Y and Z are kept one above another in a stack, such that the bottommost box is numbered 1 and the topmost box is numbered 8. Box W is kept at the third position from the top. Box T is kept three places below box W. Only one box is kept between box T and box Y. Box X is kept immediately above box Y. Only one box is kept between box X and box Z. Box U is kept above box S, and more than one box is kept between them.
**Q1.** Which box is kept at the top of the stack? (A) V (B) U (C) W (D) S (E) Z
**Q2.** How many boxes are kept between box W and box X? (A) One (B) Two (C) Three (D) Four (E) Five
**Q3.** Which box is kept immediately above box T? (A) S (B) X (C) W (D) Z (E) Y
**Conventional Method:** test orders of the eight boxes against all six clues.
**Fast Method:**
1. Third from the top of 8 is place 6: W at 6.
2. Three places below: T at 3.
3. One box between T and Y: Y at 5 or 1. X is immediately above Y; with Y at 5, X needs place 6, which W holds. So Y at 1, X at 2.
4. One box between X and Z: Z at 4.
5. Places 5, 7, 8 are left for S, U, V. U above S with more than one box between: U at 8, S at 5. V takes 7.
Stack, top to bottom: U, V, W, S, Z, T, X, Y.
**Answers:** Q1 (B). Q2 (C) Three. Q3 (D) Z.
**Option Elimination Hack:** for Q1, W is third from the top, so (C) is out; S must have U well above it, so (D) is out.
**IBPS Trap Warning:** two traps in one set. “Third from the top” is place 6 here, not place 3. And “three places below” is a difference of 3; reading it as three boxes between puts T at 2 and the set no longer has a single arrangement.
### 4. Cheat Sheet and Recall Matrix
<table header-row="true">
<tr>
<td>If you see…</td>
<td>Do this</td>
<td>Watch for</td>
</tr>
<tr>
<td>“nth from the bottom”, “only n boxes below”</td>
<td>Place n, or place n + 1</td>
<td>Off by one</td>
</tr>
<tr>
<td>“nth from the top”, “only n boxes above”</td>
<td>Convert to a bottom number first</td>
<td>Using the top number as a place</td>
</tr>
<tr>
<td>“Only n boxes between”</td>
<td>Difference n + 1; one or two places</td>
<td>Difference n</td>
</tr>
<tr>
<td>“n places above”, “n boxes above”</td>
<td>Difference n</td>
<td>Reading it as n between</td>
</tr>
<tr>
<td>“Immediately above / below”</td>
<td>Glue as a block of two</td>
<td>Top or bottom kills a case</td>
</tr>
<tr>
<td>“As many above X as below Y”</td>
<td>Mirror positions: X and Y add to N + 1</td>
<td>Same end for both</td>
</tr>
<tr>
<td>“Above”, “below” with no number</td>
<td>Keep for last</td>
<td>Treating as immediate</td>
</tr>
<tr>
<td>A colour or item per box</td>
<td>Second column, same numbers</td>
<td>Mixing name and colour clues</td>
</tr>
</table>
**Order of work, in one line:** number from the bottom, fixed clue, chain the linked clues, split into two columns, kill one, loose clues, questions.
**Useful fact.** If as many boxes are above X as below Y, then place of X + place of Y = N + 1. In a stack of 8, the pair is (1, 8), (2, 7), (3, 6) or (4, 5).

**What was checked by script**
- 40 of the 77 bank sets (20 Sreedhar, 20 Guidely, spread across the sub-sections) were solved by brute force: a script tried every arrangement against every clue as worded, and the questions were answered from what survived. The other 37 sets were sorted by section name but not solved.
- 37 of the 40 have exactly one arrangement. For 22 of those the solver noted a reading choice, most often “n boxes above” meaning n places above, and “west of” meaning the same shelf in two-stack sets.
- 3 do not: BX189 (S-MT14-87 to 91) has five arrangements; BX205 (the set containing S-MT8-70) has two, with two boxes interchangeable; BX280 (a Guidely two-stack set) has four on a plain reading and part of a question merged into its passage.
- 198 questions in those sets: 194 agree with the key; 4 cannot be settled because their set (BX189) is not unique (S-MT14-88 to 91).
- The three drill puzzles each have exactly one arrangement by script, and their answers come from the script.
**Where the paper evidence comes from.** One helper run opened the 24 PDFs once each and listed the puzzle sets in each Reasoning section. I did not re-open them to cross-check, so a set may have been missed in a paper. The counts are sightings, not frequencies.
**How the bank check was run.** The 40 sets were solved in helper runs, each writing its own solver script per puzzle. I merged and counted their results; I did not re-read each solver. The “cases” figures are the helpers’ estimate of a human path, not something computed.
**Data problems in the bank.** A few Guidely passages have lost their opening line (the list of boxes). Two Sreedhar “odd one out” questions say “based on positions in the English alphabetical order” when the key only works from the stack; treat that phrase as meaning the arrangement.
**Not verified:** the 37 unsolved bank sets; the paper inventory beyond a single reading; how often each form appears beyond these 24 papers. Memory-based papers are reconstructions.
**Corrections:** 6 Oct 2026, made while copying into this combined file: Drill 1, Fast Method step 2 read “difference 4” in the separate Box file; two boxes between means a difference of 3 (R at 3, T at 6). The answer was already right; only the wording is fixed here.
## Module 5: Linear Arrangement - Speed Mastery Module
**Sample.** This module is built mainly from your question bank, as you asked. Bank: 115 seating sets with 520 questions (83 sets where the number of persons is given, 32 where it is not; 74 sets from Guidely, 41 from Sreedhar). Your bank has no previous-year Reasoning questions, so what the exam asks comes from 24 memory-based papers opened on the web: 12 IBPS Clerk Prelims papers (2018 to Oct 2025) and 12 sister papers (SBI Clerk, IBPS RRB Clerk, IBPS PO Prelims). In the 12 Clerk papers there were 7 single-row sets, and every one of them was the form where the number of persons is not given. In the 12 sister papers there were 6 of that form and 1 with a fixed 8 persons. Memory-based papers are reconstructions, not official papers.
**A note on time.** The exam’s “certain number of persons” row comes with only 3 to 5 questions and is short: target 3 minutes for the row and 15 seconds a question. A full 8-person row with 5 questions is a 4 to 5 minute job.
### 1. Basic Type, Core Mental Model and Grid
**What the basic type looks like.** People sit in one straight row. In the basic form all face north and the number of people is given (6 to 8). Clues say who is to the left or right of whom and how many sit between. You rebuild the row.
**The form the Clerk papers actually use.** “A certain number of persons sit in a row facing north.” Only some of them are named. Clues chain the named people together with gaps, and one clue ties the chain to an end of the row. You find where each named person sits and how many people are in the row.
**The one idea: a row is a number line. Give every clue a number, and the row builds itself.**
**How to draw it**
- Known number: a line of blanks, numbered 1, 2, 3… from the left.
- Unknown number: do not draw the ends. Write the first named person in the middle of the page and grow the chain left and right, with dashes for unnamed seats. Add the ends only when a clue gives them.
**Left and right**
<table header-row="true">
<tr>
<td>The person faces</td>
<td>Their left is</td>
<td>Their right is</td>
</tr>
<tr>
<td>North</td>
<td>West (your left on the page)</td>
<td>East (your right)</td>
</tr>
<tr>
<td>South</td>
<td>East (your right on the page)</td>
<td>West (your left)</td>
</tr>
</table>
Left and right belong to the person named after “of”. When everyone faces north you can forget this table; when anyone faces south you cannot.
**The solving algorithm**
1. Draw the line. If people face different ways, write an up or down arrow under each name as you place it.
2. Known number: place the fixed clue (“third from the left end”, “at an extreme end”). Unknown number: start with the person who appears in the most clues.
3. Chain the gap clues. “Third to the right” is one seat. “Only two persons between” is two possible seats: two drawings.
4. Kill a drawing when a seat is taken twice, when someone falls off a known end, or when a “not a neighbour” clue is broken.
5. Unknown number: find the clue that mentions an end (“only two persons sit to the left of A”, “B is fourth from the right end”, “as many sit to the right of C as to the left of A”). It fixes the ends; count the seats.
6. Answer the questions.
**Grid: clues as numbers (row numbered from the left, everyone facing north)**
<table header-row="true">
<tr>
<td>Wording</td>
<td>As a number</td>
</tr>
<tr>
<td>X is third to the right of Y</td>
<td>X = Y + 3</td>
</tr>
<tr>
<td>X is third to the left of Y</td>
<td>X = Y − 3</td>
</tr>
<tr>
<td>Only two persons between X and Y</td>
<td>X = Y + 3 or Y − 3</td>
</tr>
<tr>
<td>Only two persons sit to the left of X</td>
<td>X = 3</td>
</tr>
<tr>
<td>X is fourth from the right end, row of N</td>
<td>X = N − 3</td>
</tr>
<tr>
<td>X sits exactly between Y and Z</td>
<td>X is the midpoint of Y and Z</td>
</tr>
<tr>
<td>As many to the left of X as to the right of Y</td>
<td>X − 1 = N − Y</td>
</tr>
<tr>
<td>X is nth from the left and mth from the right</td>
<td>N = n + m − 1</td>
</tr>
<tr>
<td>More than three persons between X and Y</td>
<td>Differ by at least 5</td>
</tr>
</table>
**Non-linear warning.** “Third to the right” (difference 3) and “three persons between” (difference 4) differ by one seat, and one seat off at the start moves the whole row and changes the total.
**Universal fallback (works on every set in this topic):** number the seats, write each clue as a line of arithmetic from the grid, and try each possible seat for the first person.
### 2. Archetypes
Bank counts are sets out of 115. Paper counts are sets seen in the 12 IBPS Clerk Prelims papers and in the 12 sister papers.

</tr>

<td>Confirmed</td>
</tr>

</tr>

</tr>

</tr>

The L2 to L4 bank figures are approximate: 41 Sreedhar sets are not labelled by facing direction and were sorted by words in the passage.
Where they were seen:
- Unknown number: IBPS Clerk 4 Oct 2025 Shifts 1, 3 and 4, 24 Aug 2024 Shift 1, 2022, 2021 and 2019, each with 3 to 5 questions; SBI Clerk 20 Sep 2025 Shift 1, 21 Sep 2025 and 22 Feb 2025; IBPS RRB Clerk 6 Dec 2025 Shift 2 and 13 Dec 2025 Shift 2; IBPS PO 24 Aug 2025 Shift 1.
- Known number, 8 persons: SBI Clerk 22 Feb 2025 only.
What stands out: this is the clearest gap between your bank and the papers in any puzzle topic. The bank is mostly full 8-person rows; the papers opened are almost all the short “certain number of persons” form. Learn the basic row first because the skills are the same, then spend most of your practice on L1.
What the bank sets look like (from the 40 sets solved by script, all of which have exactly one arrangement): 32 leave two cases after a good first clue, 4 leave three, 3 leave one. In the unknown-number sets the totals came out between 13 and 36 seats with 7 to 10 people named.
#### Core archetypes
**L1. Unknown number of persons. Confirmed.** *Pattern clues:* “a certain number of persons”, “not more than 20 persons”. *Shortcut engine:* build the chain of named people with dashes for gaps; find the clue that touches an end; count. *Worked example:* Drill 2 below.
**L2. Known number, all facing north. Seen once (SBI Clerk Prelims 22 Feb 2025).** *Pattern clues:* “eight persons sit in a straight row facing north”. *Shortcut engine:* numbered blanks; fixed clue; gap clues give two drawings; neighbour clue kills one. *Worked example:* Drill 1 below.
#### Insurance archetypes (Bank only)
**L3. All facing south.** Draw the row as usual, then swap the words left and right in every clue. Or turn your page upside down in your head and solve it as a north row.
**L4. Mixed facing.** Arrow under every name. For each clue, use the arrow of the person after “of”. *Worked example:* Drill 3 below.
**L5. Second fact or vacant seat.** A second line under the row for the fact. A vacant seat is a seat but not a person; in three of the bank sets solved, the clues only work when “persons between” skips the vacant seat.
### 3. Drill Set
Three original puzzles written for this module. Each was checked by script and has exactly one arrangement.
#### Drill 1 (Basic, L2: known number, facing north)
Six persons D, E, F, G, H and J are sitting in a straight row, all facing north, but not necessarily in the same order. G sits third from the left end of the row. Only one person sits between G and J. E sits to the immediate right of J. H sits at one of the extreme ends of the row. D is not an immediate neighbour of G.
**Q1.** Who sits to the immediate left of D? (A) G (B) E (C) F (D) H (E) J
**Q2.** How many persons sit between J and H? (A) One (B) Two (C) Three (D) Four (E) None
**Q3.** Who sits second to the right of G? (A) D (B) F (C) H (D) E (E) J
**Conventional Method:** try every order of the six persons against the five clues.
**Fast Method:**
1. G at seat 3.
2. One person between G and J: J at 1 or at 5.
3. E is immediately right of J: J1 E2, or J5 E6.
4. H at an end: seat 6 in the first drawing, seat 1 in the second.
5. D is not next to G. In the second drawing the free seats are 2 and 4, both next to G: it dies. In the first, free seats are 4 and 5: D at 5, F at 4.
Row, left to right: J, E, G, F, D, H.
**Answers:** Q1 (C) F. Q2 (D) Four. Q3 (A) D.
**Option Elimination Hack:** for Q3, second to the right of G is seat 5, and D cannot be at seat 2 or 4, so once J and E take 1 and 2 the answer is forced before you place F.
**IBPS Trap Warning:** “only one person between G and J” does not say which side. Trying only the right side leads to a dead end and a wrong left-end answer.
#### Drill 2 (Exam form, L1: unknown number of persons)
A certain number of persons are sitting in a straight row, all facing north. Only two persons sit to the left of A. B sits third to the right of A. Only four persons sit between B and C. D sits second to the left of C. As many persons sit to the right of C as to the left of A.
**Q1.** How many persons are sitting in the row? (A) 11 (B) 12 (C) 13 (D) 14 (E) 15
**Q2.** How many persons sit between A and D? (A) Four (B) Five (C) Six (D) Three (E) Two
**Q3.** What is the position of D from the right end of the row? (A) Third (B) Fourth (C) Sixth (D) Fifth (E) Seventh
**Conventional Method:** try each possible row length in turn and place the four people.
**Fast Method:**
1. Two persons to the left of A: A is at seat 3.
2. B is third to the right: seat 6.
3. Four persons between B and C: C at 11 or at 1.
4. D is second to the left of C. With C at 1 there is no seat to its left. So C at 11, D at 9.
5. As many to the right of C as to the left of A: two. The row ends at seat 13.
Row: seats 1 to 13, with A at 3, B at 6, D at 9, C at 11.
**Answers:** Q1 (C) 13. Q2 (B) Five. Q3 (D) Fifth.
**Option Elimination Hack:** for Q3 use the rule N = left position + right position − 1: 13 = 9 + right − 1, so right = 5. No counting on the drawing.
**IBPS Trap Warning:** “only four persons between B and C” is a difference of 5, not 4. Using 4 puts C at 10, D at 8 and makes the row 12 long, which is option (B).
#### Drill 3 (Trap, L4: mixed facing)
Eight persons A, B, C, D, E, F, G and H are sitting in a straight row which runs from west to east, but not necessarily in the same order. B, E and H face south, while all the others face north. D sits at one of the extreme ends of the row. Only two persons sit between D and E. H sits second to the left of E. Only one person sits between H and C. B sits to the immediate right of H. G sits second to the right of B. A is not an immediate neighbour of D.
**Q1.** Who sits to the immediate left of A? (A) C (B) B (C) G (D) H (E) E
**Q2.** How many persons sit between F and B? (A) One (B) Two (C) Three (D) Four (E) None
**Q3.** Who sits second to the right of G? (A) D (B) H (C) B (D) E (E) F
**Conventional Method:** try every order of the eight persons, using each person’s own left and right.
**Fast Method:**
1. Arrows: B, E, H face south, so their left is east and their right is west.
2. D at an end with two between D and E: D at 1 and E at 4, or D at 8 and E at 5.
3. H is second to the left of E; E faces south, so go two seats east: H at 6, or H at 7.
4. One person between H and C. With H at 7, C needs seat 5 (taken by E) or seat 9 (does not exist): this drawing dies. With H at 6, seat 4 is E’s, so C at 8.
5. B is immediately right of H (H faces south: one seat west): B at 5. G is second to the right of B (two seats west): G at 3.
6. Seats 2 and 7 are left. A is not next to D: A at 7, F at 2.
Row, west to east: D, F, G, E, B, H, A, C.
**Answers:** Q1 (D) H. Q2 (B) Two. Q3 (C) B.
**Option Elimination Hack:** none worth using; the questions need the finished row. Check each answer against the arrow of the person named after “of”: A and G both face north.
**IBPS Trap Warning:** for a person facing south, left is towards the east. Solving as if all eight face north gives the mirror row, and then Q1 comes out as C and Q3 as D.
### 4. Cheat Sheet and Recall Matrix
<table header-row="true">
<tr>
<td>If you see…</td>
<td>Do this</td>
<td>Watch for</td>
</tr>
<tr>
<td>“A certain number of persons”</td>
<td>Chain from the most-mentioned person; no ends yet</td>
<td>Drawing ends too early</td>
</tr>
<tr>
<td>“Only n persons sit to the left of X”</td>
<td>X is at seat n + 1; this fixes the left end</td>
<td>Seat n</td>
</tr>
<tr>
<td>“X is nth from the right end”</td>
<td>Right end is n − 1 seats beyond X</td>
<td>Off by one</td>
</tr>
<tr>
<td>“As many to the left of X as to the right of Y”</td>
<td>Ends mirror each other; gives the total</td>
<td>Using the same end twice</td>
</tr>
<tr>
<td>“nth to the left / right of”</td>
<td>One seat, exact</td>
<td>Whose left</td>
</tr>
<tr>
<td>“Only n persons between”</td>
<td>Two seats, two drawings</td>
<td>Difference n instead of n + 1</td>
</tr>
<tr>
<td>“Exactly between Y and Z”</td>
<td>Midpoint</td>
<td>Any seat between</td>
</tr>
<tr>
<td>“Facing south”</td>
<td>Swap left and right</td>
<td>Forgetting on the questions</td>
</tr>
<tr>
<td>“Not an immediate neighbour”</td>
<td>Kills a drawing</td>
<td>Using it first</td>
</tr>
</table>
**Order of work, in one line:** line, arrows if needed, anchor person, exact-gap clues, two drawings for “between” clues, kill one, end clue gives the total, questions.
**Three formulas for the unknown-number form**
1. Total = position from left + position from right − 1.
2. Persons between seats p and q = q − p − 1.
3. Persons to the right of seat p in a row of N = N − p.

## Module 6: Parallel Rows Seating - Speed Mastery Module
**Sample.** This module is built mainly from your question bank, as you asked. Bank: 62 seating sets with 300 questions (34 sets from Guidely, 28 from Sreedhar). Your bank has no previous-year Reasoning questions, so what the exam asks comes from 24 memory-based papers opened on the web: 12 IBPS Clerk Prelims papers (2018 to Oct 2025) and 12 sister papers (SBI Clerk, IBPS RRB Clerk, IBPS PO Prelims). Parallel-row sets seen: 2 in the Clerk papers (2019 and 2018, 12 persons each) and 3 in the sister papers. Memory-based papers are reconstructions, not official papers.
**A note on time.** A parallel-row set is the longest seating set in the paper: two rows to build and about five questions. The honest target is 5 to 6 minutes for the rows and 15 to 20 seconds for each question after it. If time is short, do the box and month sets first.
### 1. Basic Type, Core Mental Model and Grid
**What the basic type looks like.** Ten or twelve people sit in two rows of five or six. Row 1 faces south and Row 2 faces north (or the other way round), so each person in one row faces one person in the other. Clues tell you positions within a row and who faces whom.
**The one idea: it is two linear rows joined by “faces”. Draw both rows, fix each row’s left and right once, and let every “faces” clue carry a position straight across.**
**How to draw it.** Two lines of blanks, one above the other, columns lined up. Put the south-facing row on top and the north-facing row at the bottom, so the two rows look at each other. Then write the four words on the page:
<table header-row="true">
<tr>
<td>Row</td>
<td>Faces</td>
<td>Their left is</td>
<td>Their right is</td>
</tr>
<tr>
<td>Top row</td>
<td>South</td>
<td>Your right (east)</td>
<td>Your left (west)</td>
</tr>
<tr>
<td>Bottom row</td>
<td>North</td>
<td>Your left (west)</td>
<td>Your right (east)</td>
</tr>
</table>
Write L and R at the two ends of each row before reading any clue. In the top row the R goes on your left.
**The solving algorithm (use it on every set):**
1. Draw the two rows, with the names of each row beside it. Mark L and R at the ends of each row.
2. Place a fixed clue: “sits at the extreme left end of row 1”, “sits third from the right end”.
3. Use “faces” clues to jump to the other row: the person faced is in the same column.
4. Gap clues inside a row (“only two persons between”) give two places: draw the pair of rows twice.
5. Long gaps are gifts: in a row of five, “three persons between S and R” puts them at the two ends; in a row of six, “four persons between” does the same.
6. Kill a drawing when someone falls off the row or a seat is used twice.
7. “Does not face”, “not at an end”, “not a neighbour” clues settle the last two people.
**Grid: counting in a row of 5 or 6**
<table header-row="true">
<tr>
<td>Wording</td>
<td>Row of 5</td>
<td>Row of 6</td>
</tr>
<tr>
<td>Sits in the middle</td>
<td>Seat 3</td>
<td>No single middle seat</td>
</tr>
<tr>
<td>Three persons between X and Y</td>
<td>Seats 1 and 5</td>
<td>Seats 1 and 5, or 2 and 6</td>
</tr>
<tr>
<td>Four persons between X and Y</td>
<td>Impossible</td>
<td>Seats 1 and 6</td>
</tr>
<tr>
<td>Third to the right of X</td>
<td>X at 1 or 2 (counting from that row’s left)</td>
<td>X at 1, 2 or 3</td>
</tr>
<tr>
<td>X faces Y</td>
<td>Same column</td>
<td>Same column</td>
</tr>
<tr>
<td>X faces the one who sits second to the left of Y</td>
<td>Find Y’s column, move two towards Y’s left, go straight across</td>
<td>Same</td>
</tr>
</table>
**Non-linear warning.** The two rows have opposite lefts. A clue about a top-row person and a clue about a bottom-row person that both say “to the left” send you in opposite directions on the page.
**Universal fallback (works on every set in this topic):** solve the row with more fixed clues as a plain linear row first, listing its two or three possible orders; then test each order against the “faces” clues.

<tr>
<td>P5</td>
<td>With a vacant seat in each row, or a second fact per person</td>
<td>8</td>
<td>0</td>
<td>0</td>
<td>Bank only</td>
</tr>
</table>
The size figures come from the number word in each passage and are approximate; 12 passages did not state a size the script could read.
Where they were seen: IBPS Clerk 2019 and 2018 (12 persons, 5 questions each); SBI Clerk 20 Sep 2025 Shift 2 (10 persons); IBPS PO 22 Aug 2026 Shift 1 and 24 Aug 2025 Shift 1 (14 persons each).
What stands out: none of the six Clerk shifts from 2024 and 2025 had a parallel-row set; the two Clerk sightings are from 2018 and 2019. It is the least seen of the six puzzle topics in the Clerk papers opened. Learn it, because the same skill serves SBI Clerk and PO, but give it less practice time than month, box, circle or square.
What the bank sets look like (from the 40 sets solved by script): 5 + 5 is the most common (17 sets), then 6 + 6 (10). After a good first clue, 31 sets leave two cases, 5 leave one and 4 leave three.
#### Core archetypes
**P2. Six and six. Confirmed (IBPS Clerk Prelims 2019 and 2018).** *Pattern clues:* “twelve persons are sitting in two parallel rows containing six persons each”. *Shortcut engine:* as the algorithm above. With six seats there is no middle seat, and “four persons between” means the two ends.
**P1. Five and five. Seen once (SBI Clerk Prelims 20 Sep 2025 Shift 2).** *Pattern clues:* “ten persons… five persons each”. *Shortcut engine:* the same, and the middle seat (seat 3) is a useful anchor: “sits in the middle of the row” is a fixed clue. *Worked example:* Drill 2 below.
**P3. Seven and seven. Seen in IBPS PO only.** *Shortcut engine:* the same method; expect one more case. The middle seat is seat 4.
#### Insurance archetypes (Bank only)
**P4. Four and four.** The small form, good for learning. *Worked example:* Drill 1 below.
**P5. Vacant seats or a second fact.** A vacant seat is a seat but not a person: “persons between” skips it, and someone can face a vacant seat. *Worked example:* Drill 3 below.
### 3. Drill Set
Three original puzzles written for this module. Each was checked by a brute-force script and has exactly one arrangement. Rows are written west to east, that is, left to right on your page.
#### Drill 1 (Basic, P4: four and four)
Eight persons are sitting in two parallel rows containing four persons each, with equal distance between adjacent persons. In row 1, K, L, M and N are seated and all of them are facing north. In row 2, T, U, V and W are seated and all of them are facing south. Each person in row 1 faces exactly one person in row 2. L sits at the extreme right end of row 1. The one who faces L sits to the immediate left of W. Only one person sits between W and V. K faces T. M sits to the left of N.
**Q1.** Who faces N? (A) T (B) U (C) V (D) W (E) Cannot be determined
**Q2.** How many persons sit to the right of T? (A) None (B) One (C) Two (D) Three (E) Cannot be determined
**Q3.** Who sits second to the left of the one who faces W? (A) M (B) K (C) L (D) N (E) No one
**Conventional Method:** try every order of each row and test the clues.
**Fast Method:**
1. Row 1 faces north: its right is east. L at the east end of row 1.
2. The person facing L is at the east end of row 2 and is on W’s immediate left. Row 2 faces south, so its left is east: W is one seat west of that person, second from the east.
3. One person between W and V: going east leaves the row, so V is at the west end.
4. T and U take the other two seats of row 2. K faces T, and the east-end seat faces L, so T is second from the west and U is at the east end. K is opposite T.
5. M is to the left of N in a north-facing row: M at the west end, N third from the west.
Row 1 (facing north): M, K, N, L. Row 2 (facing south): V, T, W, U.
**Answers:** Q1 (D) W. Q2 (B) One. Q3 (A) M.
**Option Elimination Hack:** for Q1, K faces T is given, so N cannot face T: (A) is out without solving.
**IBPS Trap Warning:** row 2 faces south, so its left is east. Using row 1’s sense of left for row 2 pushes W off the row. In Q2 the same slip counts two people to T’s right instead of one.
#### Drill 2 (Exam level, P1: five and five)
Ten persons are sitting in two parallel rows containing five persons each, with equal distance between adjacent persons. In row 1, G, H, J, K and L are seated and all of them are facing south. In row 2, P, Q, R, S and T are seated and all of them are facing north. Each person in row 1 faces exactly one person in row 2. L sits third to the right of H. Only one person sits between H and G. Q faces L. Three persons sit between S and R. K faces P. J does not face R.
**Q1.** Who faces G? (A) P (B) Q (C) S (D) R (E) T
**Q2.** How many persons sit between Q and the one who faces H? (A) None (B) One (C) Two (D) Three (E) Cannot be determined
**Q3.** Who sits second to the right of K? (A) H (B) L (C) G (D) J (E) No one
**Conventional Method:** try every order of each row and test the clues.
**Fast Method:**
1. Row 1 faces south: its right is west. L is three seats west of H: H at the east end and L second from the west, or H second from the east and L at the west end.
2. One person between H and G: G in the middle (first drawing), or G second from the west (second drawing).
3. Three persons between S and R in a row of five: S and R are at the two ends of row 2. Q faces L, so L is not at an end. The second drawing dies.
4. J and K take the west end and the fourth seat of row 1. K faces P, and P is not at an end, so K is fourth from the west with P opposite; J is at the west end.
5. T takes the middle of row 2, facing G.
6. J does not face R: S is at the west end facing J, R at the east end facing H.
Row 1 (facing south): J, L, G, K, H. Row 2 (facing north): S, Q, T, P, R.
**Answers:** Q1 (E) T. Q2 (C) Two. Q3 (B) L.
**Option Elimination Hack:** for Q1, Q faces L and P faces K by the clues, so (A) and (B) are out; S and R are at the ends and G is in the middle, so (C) and (D) are out too. Q1 can be answered after step 3.
**IBPS Trap Warning:** the slip here comes at the question stage. K faces south, so K’s right is west on your page. Reading it as east finds only H on that side and gives “No one”.
#### Drill 3 (Trap, P5: a vacant seat in each row)
Ten persons are sitting in two parallel rows containing six seats each, with equal distance between adjacent seats. One seat is vacant in each row. In row 1, D, E, F, G and H are seated and all of them are facing south. In row 2, V, W, X, Y and Z are seated and all of them are facing north. Each seat in row 1 faces exactly one seat in row 2. F sits at the extreme left end of row 1. The seat to the immediate right of W is vacant. Z is an immediate neighbour of W and faces the vacant seat of row 1. Only one person sits between W and Y. V sits to the immediate left of Y. D faces Y. E sits second to the left of G.
**Q1.** Who faces the vacant seat of row 2? (A) D (B) E (C) G (D) F (E) H
**Q2.** How many persons sit between Z and V? (A) One (B) Two (C) Three (D) None (E) Cannot be determined
**Q3.** Who faces the one who sits second to the right of E? (A) X (B) Z (C) W (D) V (E) The seat is vacant
**Conventional Method:** treat each vacant seat as a sixth name in its row and try every order.
**Fast Method:**
1. Row 1 faces south: its left is east. F at the east end of row 1.
2. Row 2 faces north: the seat just east of W is vacant, and Z, W’s neighbour, is just west of W. Block: Z, W, vacant.
3. One person between W and Y, with V just west of Y. Either Y is to the east (Z, W, vacant, V, Y: V is the one person) or to the west (V, Y, Z, W, vacant: Z is the one person). Each block is five seats long and can start at seat 1 or seat 2.
4. D faces Y, and the east end of row 1 is F’s, so Y is not at the east end. That removes one position. In the block Z, W, vacant, V, Y starting at seat 1, D is fifth from the west and row 1’s vacant seat is at the west end, facing Z.
5. E is second to the left of G: E is two seats east of G. The free seats of row 1 are the second, third and fourth: G second, E fourth, H third. In the other block the free seats never leave a pair two apart, so it dies.
6. X takes the east end of row 2.
Row 1 (facing south): vacant, G, H, E, D, F. Row 2 (facing north): Z, W, vacant, V, Y, X.
**Answers:** Q1 (E) H. Q2 (A) One. Q3 (C) W.
**Option Elimination Hack:** for Q1, D faces Y and F is at the end facing a person, so (A) and (D) are out.
**IBPS Trap Warning:** “only one person sits between W and Y” counts persons, not seats. Two seats lie between them and one is vacant. The same trap is in Q2: two seats between Z and V, but one person.
### 4. Cheat Sheet and Recall Matrix
<table header-row="true">
<tr>
<td>If you see…</td>
<td>Do this</td>
<td>Watch for</td>
</tr>
<tr>
<td>“Row 1 faces south, row 2 faces north”</td>
<td>South row on top; L and R marked at each row’s ends</td>
<td>Same left for both rows</td>
</tr>
<tr>
<td>“X faces Y”</td>
<td>Same column</td>
<td>Diagonal</td>
</tr>
<tr>
<td>“Extreme end”, “middle of the row”</td>
<td>Fixed clue, place first</td>
<td>Which end is that row’s left</td>
</tr>
<tr>
<td>“n persons between” with n = seats − 2</td>
<td>The two ends</td>
<td>Missing this gift</td>
</tr>
<tr>
<td>“X faces the one who is second to the right of Y”</td>
<td>Y’s column, move by Y’s right, go across</td>
<td>Using X’s right</td>
</tr>
<tr>
<td>“X does not face Y”, “not at an end”</td>
<td>Keep for the last two people</td>
<td>Using it first</td>
</tr>
<tr>
<td>Vacant seat</td>
<td>A seat, not a person</td>
<td>Counting it as a person</td>
</tr>
</table>
**Order of work, in one line:** two rows, L and R marks, fixed clue, faces clues across, end-to-end gaps, two drawings, kill one, loose clues, questions.
**One check before each answer.** Which row is the person after “of” in? Use that row’s left and right.

### 6. Verification Log
**Built:** 6 Oct 2026.
**What was checked by script**
- 40 of the 62 bank sets (20 Sreedhar, 20 Guidely) were solved by brute force: a script tried every seating of both rows against every clue as worded, and the questions were answered from what survived. The other 22 sets were sorted by section name but not solved.
- 36 of the 40 have exactly one arrangement. For 14 of those the solver noted a reading choice, most often “to the left of” meaning anywhere to the left, and “persons between” skipping a vacant seat.
- 4 do not: PR099 and PR115 fix every seat but leave one person’s facing direction open (all their questions still have one answer); PR227 has two arrangements; PR572 (Guidely parallel row with vacant, set 1, questions 6 to 10) has lost the opening lines of its passage and cannot be solved.
- 200 questions in those sets: 195 agree with the key; 5 cannot be settled (the PR572 questions).
- Two Guidely sets in the sample are the same puzzle with the same questions.
- The three drill puzzles each have exactly one arrangement by script, and their answers come from the script.
**Where the paper evidence comes from.** One helper run opened the 24 PDFs once each and listed the puzzle sets in each Reasoning section. I did not re-open them to cross-check, so a set may have been missed in a paper. The counts are sightings, not frequencies.
**How the bank check was run.** The 40 sets were solved in helper runs, each writing its own solver script per puzzle. I merged and counted their results; I did not re-read each solver. The “cases” figures are the helpers’ estimate of a human path.
**Not verified:** the 22 unsolved bank sets; the size split of the bank sets; the paper inventory beyond a single reading. Memory-based papers are reconstructions.
**Corrections:** none so far.

## Module 11: Direction Sense - Speed Mastery Module
**Sample.** Papers: I opened the 35 cached memory-based papers (23 IBPS Clerk Prelims files, 16 of them with a readable Reasoning section, and 12 sister papers: SBI Clerk, IBPS RRB Clerk, IBPS PO Prelims). Paper instances: 13 direction sets with 37 questions. IBPS Clerk: 12 Dec 2021 (three reconstructions: Guidely set 1, Guidely set 2, Adda247), 2023 (Guidely), 2025 (Guidely), 4 Oct 2025 shift 1 and 4 Oct 2025 shift 3 (Adda247): 7 sets. Sister: SBI Clerk 20 Sep 2025 shift 1 and 21 Sep 2025 shift 1, IBPS RRB Clerk 13 Dec 2025 shift 1, IBPS PO 23 Aug 2025 shift 2, 24 Aug 2025 shift 1 and 22 Aug 2026 shift 1: 6 sets. Bank: all 48 Sreedhar sets (41 distinct; 7 are reprints of another set) and both Guidely sets (13 short passages), 173 questions, of which 171 were solved by script with coordinates; all 171 agree with the bank key. Floor: the 6 sister sets meet the 6 to 10 sister floor; the Clerk side is below the floor of 8 shifts, because the three 12 Dec 2021 sets may come from the same day's shifts and the Guidely 2025 paper may overlap an Adda247 shift. The web shift analyses (adda247, careerpower, pw.live, time4education) could not be opened from here; one Oliveboard page gave 4 Oct 2025 shift 1 as "Direction & Distance, 3 questions", which matches the paper. Memory-based papers are reconstructions, not official papers.
**A note on time.** A direction set is one passage followed by two or three questions. The honest target is about 60 seconds to place the points and 15 to 20 seconds for each question after that.
### 1. Basic Type, Core Mental Model and Grid
**What the basic type looks like.** Six to nine points on flat ground. Either a walk ("starts from A, walks 6m west to B, takes a left turn ...") or a network of clues ("Point E is 7m north of point F"). The questions ask the direction of one point from another, the shortest distance between two points, or which pair is the odd one out.
**The one idea: put the first point at (0, 0). East is plus and west is minus for the first number; north is plus and south is minus for the second. Every clue then adds a few metres to one of the two numbers.**
**Clue to coordinates**
<table header-row="true">
<tr>
<td>The clue says</td>
<td>Write</td>
<td>Note</td>
</tr>
<tr>
<td>P is 5m north of Q</td>
<td>P = Q + (0, 5)</td>
<td>Q must already be placed; if only P is placed, go backwards</td>
</tr>
<tr>
<td>P is 5m west of Q</td>
<td>P = Q + (-5, 0)</td>
<td></td>
</tr>
<tr>
<td>X is east of P and south of Q (no distance)</td>
<td>X = (east number of Q, north number of P)</td>
<td>The corner where a row meets a column</td>
</tr>
<tr>
<td>M is exactly in the middle of A and B</td>
<td>Average both numbers</td>
<td>Halves are fine</td>
</tr>
<tr>
<td>Walker facing N, E, S, W takes a right turn</td>
<td>Faces E, S, W, N</td>
<td>One step clockwise</td>
</tr>
<tr>
<td>Walker facing N, E, S, W takes a left turn</td>
<td>Faces W, N, E, S</td>
<td>One step anticlockwise</td>
</tr>
<tr>
<td>"walks 9m west" in the middle of a walk</td>
<td>A compass word, not a turn</td>
<td>Ignore the old facing</td>
</tr>
</table>
**Answering**
<table header-row="true">
<tr>
<td>Question</td>
<td>Do this</td>
</tr>
<tr>
<td>Direction of X with respect to Y</td>
<td>X minus Y. One gap zero: N, S, E or W. Both gaps non-zero: a corner (North-East and so on), whatever their sizes</td>
</tr>
<tr>
<td>Shortest distance X to Y</td>
<td>Square both gaps, add, take the root. Same row or column: just the gap</td>
</tr>
<tr>
<td>Total distance walked</td>
<td>Add the legs</td>
</tr>
<tr>
<td>Odd pair out</td>
<td>Direction of the second point from the first, for each option</td>
</tr>
<tr>
<td>Three points in a straight line</td>
<td>Same east number (column) or same north number (row)</td>
</tr>
</table>
**Right-angle shapes to know:** 3-4-5, 6-8-10, 9-12-15, 12-16-20, 15-20-25, 5-12-13, 8-15-17. Most bank and paper distances are one of these.
**Non-linear warning.** Distances do not add along a bend. Gaps of 6 and 8 give 10, not 14, and the 14 is usually an option. The walking distance and the shortest distance are different questions.
**Universal fallback (works on every set here):** write every point's two numbers in a short list before answering anything. All 171 bank questions and all 37 paper questions in this sample were answered by a script doing exactly that.
### 2. Archetypes
Bank counts are distinct sets (reprints removed) and questions. Paper counts are sets seen in the 7 IBPS Clerk sets and the 6 sister sets listed in the sample.

DS3, DS4 and DS5 are question forms inside DS1 and DS2 passages, so they overlap those rows. DS5 is Confirmed on sister papers only (RRB Clerk 13 Dec 2025, SBI Clerk 20 Sep 2025, IBPS PO 22 Aug 2026).
Where they were seen:
- Walks: IBPS Clerk 12 Dec 2021 (Guidely set 2, rides in km; Adda247), IBPS Clerk 2025 (Guidely); RRB Clerk 13 Dec 2025 shift 1, IBPS PO 23 Aug 2025 shift 2, IBPS PO 22 Aug 2026 shift 1.
- Networks: IBPS Clerk 12 Dec 2021 (Guidely set 1), 2023 (Guidely), 4 Oct 2025 shifts 1 and 3; SBI Clerk 20 Sep 2025 and 21 Sep 2025, IBPS PO 24 Aug 2025 shift 1.
- Paper question kinds (37): direction 14, distance 11, odd pair 7, total distance 3, distance and direction together 1, "which point is not north-east" 1.
What the bank looks like (154 distinct questions): distance 65, direction 56, odd pair 13, total 7, distance and direction 4, straight line 4, other 5. Of 56 direction answers, 47 are corners. Of 69 distance answers, 6 keep a root and 8 say "approximately".
#### Core archetypes
**DS1. Single walk with turns. Confirmed.** *Pattern clues:* "starts walking", "takes a left turn", "turns right and walks". *Shortcut engine:* write the facing above each leg (N, E, S, W; right is one step clockwise), then each leg is a point clue. *Worked example:* Drill 2 below.
**DS2. Point network. Confirmed.** *Pattern clues:* "Point E is 7m north of point F", often with clues out of order. *Shortcut engine:* start from the point named in the most clues, place points in the order the chain allows, go backwards when only the first-named point is known. *Worked example:* Drill 1 below.
**DS3. Odd pair out. Confirmed.** *Pattern clues:* "Four of the following five pairs are alike based on their directions". *Shortcut engine:* direction of the second point from the first for two options; that is the common direction; find the one that breaks it, often a pure East or South among corners. *Worked example:* IBPS Clerk 4 Oct 2025 shift 3, Q93: W-N, S-V, P-G and Q-V are South-East, G-N is North-East.
**DS4. Point with no distance or added in the question. Confirmed.** *Pattern clues:* "east of W and south of R", "exactly in the middle", "If point H is 5m south of S". *Shortcut engine:* a corner takes one number from each point; a midpoint averages; an added point is used for that question only. *Worked example:* Drill 6 below.
**DS5. Total against shortest. Confirmed.** *Pattern clues:* "total distance covered / walked / ridden". *Shortcut engine:* the word total means add legs; everything else is a straight line. *Worked example:* Drill 2, the third question.
#### Insurance archetypes (Bank only)
**DS6. Two walkers.** Walk the first person to the shared place; walk the second from their own (0, 0), note how far the end is from their start, and subtract that from the shared place.
**DS7. Coded symbols.** Read the key twice: once for the direction, once for what happens to the number (in the bank: subtract 4, subtract 3, add 2, or as given). Rewrite every coded clue in words before placing anything.
**DS8. Turns only and facing.** Rights minus lefts, remainder after dividing by 4: 1 one right, 2 an about-turn, 3 one left. "Facing west, he moves towards his right" means north.
Shadows and sunrise (DS9, Unverified): in the morning the sun is in the east and shadows fall west; in the evening shadows fall east. No such question was found in the bank or in the papers opened; this is general knowledge only.
### 3. Drill Set
Six original questions written for this module. Every answer was computed by a coordinate script.
#### Drill 1 (Basic, DS2: point network)
Point B is 6m north of point A. Point C is 8m east of point B. Point D is 2m south of point C. Point E is 5m west of point D.
**Q1.** What is the shortest distance and direction of point E with respect to point A? (A) 5m North-East (B) 7m North-East (C) 5m North-West (D) 10m North-East (E) 4m North
**Conventional Method:** draw the four clues to scale and measure.
**30-Second Exam Method:** A (0, 0), B (0, 6), C (8, 6), D (8, 4), E (3, 4). E minus A: 3 east, 4 north. Corner: North-East. 3-4-5: 5m.
**Answer:** (A) 5m North-East.
**Option Elimination Hack:** both gaps are non-zero, so the answer is a corner: (E) is out. A to C is the 6-8-10 shape, so (D) is the distance to C, not E.
**IBPS Trap Warning:** adding the gaps gives 7m, option (B). Distance never adds along a bend.
#### Drill 2 (Exam level, DS1 and DS5: walk with turns)
Kiran walks 10m south from point P to point Q, turns left and walks 7m to point R, turns left and walks 4m to point S, then turns right and walks 5m to point T.
**Q1.** What is the shortest distance between P and T? (A) 6√5m (B) 12m (C) 26m (D) 4√5m (E) 13m
**Q2.** In which direction is T with respect to P? (A) North-East (B) South-East (C) South-West (D) East (E) North-West
**Q3.** What is the total distance walked by Kiran? (A) 20m (B) 6√5m (C) 26m (D) 22m (E) 30m
**Conventional Method:** draw each leg, then measure P to T.
**30-Second Exam Method:** facings S, E (left of south), N (left of east), E (right of north). P (0, 0), Q (0, -10), R (7, -10), S (7, -6), T (12, -6). T minus P: 12 east, 6 south. √(144 + 36) = √180 = 6√5. Total 10 + 7 + 4 + 5 = 26.
**Answers:** Q1 (A) 6√5m. Q2 (B) South-East. Q3 (C) 26m.
**Option Elimination Hack:** for Q1, 26 is the total walk, so (C) is out at once, and 12 is only the east-west gap. 6√5 is about 13.4, so 13m (E) is a near miss: when the options carry roots, the exact root is wanted.
**IBPS Trap Warning:** a left turn while facing south sends the walker east, not west. Taking the page's left gives T at (-12, -6) and the answer South-West.
#### Drill 3 (Exam level, DS7: coded symbols)
P $ Q (n) means P is (n - 3)m north of Q. P # Q (n) means P is (n - 3)m east of Q. P @ Q (n) means P is (n - 3)m south of Q. Statements: A $ B (8), C # B (15), D @ C (7).
**Q1.** What is the distance between A and C? (A) 17m (B) 13m (C) 12m (D) 7m (E) √119m
**Conventional Method:** decode every statement, draw, measure.
**30-Second Exam Method:** subtract 3 from every number first. A is 5m north of B; C is 12m east of B; D is 4m south of C. B (0, 0), A (0, 5), C (12, 0). 5-12-13.
**Answer:** (B) 13m.
**Option Elimination Hack:** with gaps 5 and 12 the answer is more than 12 and less than 17; only (B) fits.
**IBPS Trap Warning:** using the numbers in brackets as they stand gives gaps 8 and 15 and the answer 17m, option (A).
#### Drill 4 (Trap, DS6: two walkers)
Mohan walks 5m west from his house, turns right and walks 9m to the temple. Sohan walks 3m north from his house, turns left and walks 7m to the same temple.
**Q1.** What is the distance and direction of Sohan's house with respect to Mohan's house? (A) 2√10m North-East (B) 8m North-East (C) 2√10m North-West (D) 6m North (E) √52m North-East
**Conventional Method:** draw Mohan's walk, then try to fit Sohan's walk onto the temple.
**30-Second Exam Method:** Mohan's house (0, 0), temple (-5, 9). Sohan's temple is 7 west and 3 north of his house, so his house is the temple plus 7 east and 3 south: (2, 6). Gaps 2 and 6: √40 = 2√10, North-East.
**Answer:** (A) 2√10m North-East.
**Option Elimination Hack:** Sohan's house is east of Mohan's (2 > 0), so (C) and (D) are out.
**IBPS Trap Warning:** subtracting Sohan's walk the wrong way round puts his house at (-12, 12), North-West.
#### Drill 5 (Basic, DS8: turns only)
Neha starts walking towards the west. She takes a right turn, then two consecutive left turns, then a right turn, and finally a left turn.
**Q1.** In which direction is she walking now? (A) North (B) South (C) East (D) West (E) North-West
**Conventional Method:** turn one step at a time: W, N, W, S, W, S.
**30-Second Exam Method:** 2 rights and 3 lefts leave one left. One left from west is south.
**Answer:** (B) South.
**Option Elimination Hack:** after quarter turns only, the answer can never be a corner: (E) is out.
**IBPS Trap Warning:** a right turn while facing west is north, not east.
#### Drill 6 (Exam level, DS4: corner point and an added point)
Point Q is 12m east of point P. Point R is 9m north of point Q. Point S is 4m west of point R. Point T is south of S and east of P. Point U is 3m south of T.
**Q1.** What is the shortest distance and direction of point R with respect to point U? (A) 4√10m North-East (B) 16m North-East (C) 12m North (D) 4√10m South-West (E) 13m North-East
**Conventional Method:** draw, guess where T is, measure.
**30-Second Exam Method:** P (0, 0), Q (12, 0), R (12, 9), S (8, 9). T takes S's column and P's row: (8, 0). U (8, -3). R minus U: 4 east, 12 north. √160 = 4√10, North-East.
**Answer:** (A) 4√10m North-East.
**Option Elimination Hack:** R is above and to the right of U, so (C) and (D) are out; 16 = 4 + 12 adds the gaps, so (B) is out.
**IBPS Trap Warning:** (D) is U with respect to R, the question turned round.

**Order of work, in one line:** first point at (0, 0), list every point's two numbers, then each question is a subtraction, a root or a sum.
**Two checks before you answer.** Is it "X with respect to Y" or the other way round? Does the question say "total"?

### 6. Verification Log
**Built:** 6 Oct 2026.
**What was checked by script**
- Every direction-sense question in the banks was written as coordinates in a small Python script (each clue is "P is d metres in direction D of Q"; walks turn left or right from the current facing; corner points, midpoints and points added in a question are separate rules). Sreedhar: 133 questions in 48 sets; Guidely: 38 of 40 questions. All 171 agree with the bank key.
- Reading choices the script had to make: "approximately" means the nearest whole option (8 questions); "total distance in between Saba's initial point and Roma's initial point" (S-MT47-91 and its reprint S-MT59-91) is keyed as the whole path walked by both, 42m, not the straight 19.2m; "her friend's house is in line with her home" (S-MT42-90) was read as due north of home; "how many points are north-east of Z" (S-MT10-73) counts points strictly north and east, and leaves out the point added in another question; "Z is 25m south-west of L and south of K" (S-MT28-93) is south and west, not 45 degrees.
- Not solved: Guidely set 2, questions 16 and 17, which give no distances at all ("V is north of Q", "L is south-east of M").
- Paper sets: the 13 sets (37 questions) were solved the same way. 30 answers agree with the paper's own key, 6 had no key in the file (IBPS Clerk 12 Dec 2021 Adda247 and 4 Oct 2025 shift 1), and 1 does not agree: SBI Clerk 21 Sep 2025 Q88, where the clues as remembered give √109m for G to H but the key is 6m. In the 4 Oct 2025 shift 1 paper one clue reads "Point D is 12m east of point D"; I read it as "of point C".
- Twice a paper's "total distance between X and Y" was the path along the clues (SBI Clerk 20 Sep 2025 Q76, 60m; IBPS PO 22 Aug 2026 Q11, 40m). The script agrees with both keys on that reading.
- The six drills and the nine lesson checks were computed by the same script.
**Where the paper evidence comes from.** The cached pdftotext files of the 35 papers, searched for direction words, turn words, "shadow" and coded "means ... north" clues. Two-column layouts and one bilingual paper were read by hand from the text. The 5 Oct 2025 shift 1 paper is in Hindi with garbled text and could not be searched. Several Clerk files are Quant-only, so the 16 with Reasoning are the real base.
**Web.** WebFetch was refused for adda247.com, careerpower.in, pw.live, oliveboard.in and time4education.com; plain downloads worked for oliveboard.in, bankersadda.com, testbook.com and guidely.in. Of those, only the Oliveboard page had a usable prelims table (4 Oct 2025 shift 1: Direction & Distance, 3 questions). The 2024 Clerk shifts could not be checked from the web.
**Not verified:** how often direction sets appear across all 2024 to 2026 shifts; whether the three 12 Dec 2021 reconstructions are different shifts; shadows and sunrise (no question found anywhere). Memory-based papers are reconstructions.
**Corrections:** none so far.
## Module 9: Triangular Arrangement - Speed Mastery Module
**Sample.** This module is built from your question bank, because the papers did not have this puzzle. Papers opened: 28 memory-based papers with a Reasoning section (16 IBPS Clerk Prelims, 2016 to Oct 2025; 12 sister papers: SBI Clerk, IBPS RRB Clerk and IBPS PO Prelims, 2025 to 2026), plus 14 shift-analysis pages that list each shift's puzzles (IBPS Clerk 2023, 2024 and 2025; SBI Clerk 2025; IBPS RRB Clerk 2024 and 2025). Triangular sets found in them: 0, in clerk and sister papers alike. Bank: 31 triangular puzzles with 153 questions (24 Guidely puzzles in 6 sets, 7 Sreedhar sets; 28 different puzzles, because three are printed twice), all solved by script. Floor (8 target-exam shifts plus 6 to 10 sister questions): not met, and it cannot be met for a puzzle that was not set. Every archetype is Bank only. Memory-based papers are reconstructions, not official papers.
**A note on time.** A triangular set is a small seating set: six or nine seats and about five questions. The honest target is 3 to 4 minutes for the arrangement and 15 to 20 seconds for each question after it. Since no clerk paper opened had one, give it less practice time than circles, squares and rows.
### 1. Basic Type, Core Mental Model and Grid
**What the basic type looks like.** Six persons sit round a triangular table: three at the corners and three in the middle of the sides. Clues say who sits to the left or right of whom, who sits at a corner, and how many sit between two people. In some sets the corner persons face outside and the middle persons face the centre.
**The one idea: it is a circle of six whose seats take turns, corner, middle, corner, middle. Number the seats 1 to 6 clockwise from the top corner; the odd seats are corners and the even seats are middles, and every clue becomes a step of so many seats.**
**How to draw it**
- Six seats: a triangle with one corner at the top. Seats 1, 3, 5 are corners; 2, 4, 6 are middles.
- Nine seats: a corner and two persons on each side. Seats 1, 4, 7 are corners; the sides are 2-3, 5-6, 8-9.
- Sides only: two or three persons on each side and no one at a corner. Sides 1-2, 3-4, 5-6 (or 1-2-3, 4-5-6, 7-8-9).
**Left and right: the only rule that matters**
<table header-row="true">
<tr>
<td>The person faces</td>
<td>Their left is</td>
<td>Their right is</td>
</tr>
<tr>
<td>The centre</td>
<td>+1 (clockwise)</td>
<td>-1 (anticlockwise)</td>
</tr>
<tr>
<td>Outside</td>
<td>-1 (anticlockwise)</td>
<td>+1 (clockwise)</td>
</tr>
</table>
Left and right always belong to the person named **after** the word "of". When corners and middles face opposite ways, write OUT beside seats 1, 3, 5 and IN beside 2, 4, 6 (or the reverse) before reading a clue.
**The solving algorithm (use it on every set):**
1. Draw the table and number the seats. Mark IN or OUT.
2. Seat the person with a seat-type clue first: "X sits at a corner" goes to seat 1, "X sits in the middle of a side" to seat 2. All corners look alike, so no case is lost. A facing clue ("X faces away from the centre") is a seat-type clue when the facing goes by seat type.
3. Take a clue that joins that person to one more. "Second to the left" fixes one seat; "one person between" gives two seats: draw twice.
4. Use odd and even steps to kill a case at once.
5. "Opposite", "the same number between" and neighbour clues settle the rest.
6. Fill the last seats, then answer.
**Grid: counting on the six-seat triangle**
<table header-row="true">
<tr>
<td>Wording</td>
<td>Seats away</td>
<td>Seat type</td>
</tr>
<tr>
<td>Immediate left / right</td>
<td>1</td>
<td>Changes</td>
</tr>
<tr>
<td>Second to the left / right, one person between</td>
<td>2</td>
<td>Same</td>
</tr>
<tr>
<td>Third to the left / right, two persons between, opposite</td>
<td>3</td>
<td>Changes: a corner faces the middle of the far side</td>
</tr>
<tr>
<td>Second to the left = fourth to the right</td>
<td>2 one way, 4 the other</td>
<td>The two always add up to 6</td>
</tr>
</table>
**Grid: the nine-seat triangle**
<table header-row="true">
<tr>
<td>Step from a corner</td>
<td>Lands on</td>
</tr>
<tr>
<td>1 or 2</td>
<td>A side seat</td>
</tr>
<tr>
<td>3</td>
<td>The next corner</td>
</tr>
<tr>
<td>4 or 5</td>
<td>A side seat on the far side</td>
</tr>
<tr>
<td>6</td>
<td>The corner after next</td>
</tr>
</table>
**Non-linear warning.** For a person facing outside, left and right swap. On a table where corners face out and middles face in, "immediate left" of a corner person and "immediate left" of a middle person point in opposite directions round the table. Treating everyone as facing the centre does not give a slightly wrong answer; in Drill 2 below it gives no arrangement at all.
**Universal fallback (works on every set in this topic):** seat one person, then for the next most-mentioned person try each free seat in turn and run every clue. With six seats that is at most five small trials.
